Added 2026-08-22, measured.** [The multi-provider batch](The%20multi-provider%20batch%20%E2%80%94%20the%20surface%20that%20did%20not%20vary,%20the%20Debian%20route%20that%20exists,%20and%20the%20cards%20a%20slice%20cannot%20see.md): the same loop against the same server ran at **99–102 KB/s against a 384 KB/s cap** when each chunk waited a full round trip for its reply — one 261 120-byte write per round trip, regardless of the cap — and at **384.0 KB/s exactly** with the writes pipelined. **A synchronous write path makes the cap not the binding constraint at all**: the transfer is latency-bound, the user's chosen limit does nothing, and the rate is a property of the distance to the machine. **R6 above bounds the accounting interval and says nothing about this**, and the two are independent. **One consequence travels to [S10a](#74-resume-never-trusts-a-partial-it-has-not-verified)**: a pipelined loop counts a chunk when it hands it over, which is why a dropped connection leaves the record ahead of the disk. **The read path of a download is held to the same rule. Added 2026-09-30, on the build runner's ruling after step 10c's eleventh pass.** A download that waits a full round trip for each 261 120-byte read is bound by the distance to the machine exactly as the synchronous write path was: at 0.2 s a round trip one 50 MB file came down in 48.5 s against 7.9 s up (transfer piece 13, at the desk), and a staged prefix was verified at 1.7 MB/s on a line carrying 11. So a download keeps several reads in flight, and so does the read-back that verifies a staged prefix (S12). **What the pipelined write path costs S10a has a read-side counterpart**, which the builder reads and states: a byte is counted when it is written to the staging file, never when its read was sent, and a checkpoint covers only bytes written without a gap from the start.

**R6b. What the rate is averaged over is bounded too, and bounding the consultation interval is not the same thing. Added 2026-08-22, measured twice in each direction, and this is a correction rather than an addition.** [8.3](#83-a-cap-reaches-transfers-already-running-which-is-row-306) requires that a cap reach transfers already running. **It does — but only if the window the achieved rate is measured over is bounded**, and R6 bounds only how often the budget is consulted. A governor accounting over the **whole run** — total bytes against total elapsed — settles a changed cap against every byte already moved:

| governor | cap change | what the transfer did |
|---|---|---|
| **cumulative** | 384 → 512 | burst to about **1 000 KB/s**, roughly **double the new cap**, and held there for minutes |
| **cumulative** | 512 → 384 | fell to about **97 KB/s**, a **quarter of the new cap**, and held there |
| **five-second window** | 384 → 768 | **572.8 KB/s** across the minute the change landed in — the mean of old and new — then **704.0** |
| **five-second window** | 768 → 256 | **338.1** across the minute it landed in, then **257.1** — the new cap |

**The burst is the serious half.** A cap exists to protect the user's own link, and a cumulative governor answers a raised cap by ignoring the cap entirely for a stretch proportional to how long the transfer has already been running. **So the achieved rate every enforcement decision is taken against is measured over a bounded trailing window and never over the transfer's life**, and [R12a](#84-the-achieved-rate-reading)'s trailing three seconds is exactly that shape — its *"no decay, no smoothing, no average of an earlier average"* is what forbids the cumulative form. **Bounding how often the budget is consulted is not the same as bounding what the rate is averaged over, and only the second makes a cap change take effect now.**

**R7. A channel that drew nothing waits and does not spin, and a channel that finished returns its unused share at once.** Returning it is the half Fact 75 measured as forfeited; without it the budget shrinks over the life of a batch of uneven files, which is exactly the 421.9-against-800 result.

### 8.3 A cap reaches transfers already running, which is Row 306

**R8. Yes. A cap set or changed while transfers are in flight takes effect on each running channel at that channel's next accounting interval.** No transfer is restarted, cancelled, re-queued or re-staged for it, and no channel is closed and reopened. That follows from R6: the budget is read at each interval, so there is no moment at which a running channel holds a rate it was given at the start.

**R9. Four things that answer follows from, so a builder can see it is not a preference. Corrected 2026-08-22 — this listed three, and the run proved the three insufficient.** The budget is one shared object and not a per-transfer allowance (R1, R3). It is consulted per interval and not per transfer (R6). The interval is bounded above by R6's fixed short period — in time, and not only in bytes — so *at the next interval* is a bounded delay rather than an open one. **And the window the achieved rate is measured over is bounded too ([R6b](#82-the-enforcement-point-is-smoozys-own-byte-loop-and-the-evidence-forced-it)), which is the one this list was missing.** With the first three in place and the fourth absent, a cap raised mid-flight was answered by a **burst to roughly double the new cap for minutes**, and a cap lowered by a fall to a quarter of it — because a governor averaging over the transfer's whole life settles the change against every byte already moved. **Three of these make the change *arrive*; only the fourth makes it *take effect now*.**

**R10. What the answer does not claim, stated exactly because Row 306 decides what the user is shown.** A cap lowered mid-transfer **cannot claw back bytes already sent**, so it governs from the moment it is set forward, and an achieved-rate reading taken over a window that spans the change reads between the two rates rather than at either. **A cap raised mid-transfer is taken up as fast as the channels can use it**, which for the download direction is up to the two-to-four-stream knee (Fact 25) and not instantly. Neither is a place to look, something to type, or a thing that can be lost; [Section 13](#13-moments-that-reached-the-creator) records that this was checked.

**R11. And a caution about the unit, because the reading and the cap must agree.** Three units are already in play across the evidence and the tools: the standard tool's flag is documented in **Kbit/s**, Fact 25's throughput figures are in **MB/s**, and Fact 75's cap figures are in **KB/s**. A bit and a byte are a factor of eight apart and a kilo and a mega a factor of a thousand. **Nothing in this document carries a rate without its unit**, and the budget, the enforcement and the reading of [8.4](#84-the-achieved-rate-reading) are all in one unit fixed by the protocol version (P2). **That unit is bytes per second.** The reason is that every quantity this document's own mechanism holds is already a byte count — the negotiated maximum write of 261,120 bytes, the write chunk, the allowance drawn at each accounting interval, [S9](#73-the-progress-record-is-on-the-desktop-side-in-both-directions)'s high-water offset, [G4](#102-what-each-kind-of-work-has-to-report-and-from-where)'s bytes staged against bytes expected — so bytes per second is the one unit the enforcement point holds without a conversion at every interval. **What unit the control shows the user is `Desktop app and delivery`'s and Design's**, and a conversion at that edge is one conversion in one place.

### 8.4 The achieved-rate reading

**R12. The achieved rate is Smoozy's own count of bytes moved over a window, taken at the same accounting point as the enforcement, per channel, and summed.** It is never read out of a tool's output, and never derived from a file's size and a start time.

**R12a. The window is fixed here and it is the trailing three seconds. Added 2026-08-21, per Row 329.** [R12](#84-the-achieved-rate-reading) says *over a window* and names no length, and the length is the whole of whether the figure is steady enough to be read against the cap beside it.

- **What it is: the bytes the accounting point counted in the last three seconds, divided by three.** Nothing else enters the computation — no decay, no smoothing, no average of an earlier average — so no rate from before a cap change is carried past the bound R10 sets.
- **One length, for every channel and both directions.** The per-channel counts are summed ([R12](#84-the-achieved-rate-reading)) and a sum over windows of different lengths is not a rate.
- **The lower bound is [R6](#82-the-enforcement-point-is-smoozys-own-byte-loop-and-the-evidence-forced-it)'s and needs nothing added.** The loop emits its count *"at least once per a fixed short period whatever the chunk size"* and R6's own conclusion is that the budget is consulted *"many times per second per channel"*, so a three-second window holds many tens of counts and no single write can move the figure. ~~**That is an argument from R6's own sample count and not an observation, said here so it is not read as one — added 2026-08-23.** The only bounded window ever watched was **five seconds** ([R16](#85-what-is-not-observed-and-it-is-named-here-rather-than-in-a-footnote), [Section 12](#12-unresolved) item 14), and what that run vindicated is the trailing-window **shape** against the cumulative one, never this length. **No reading has been drawn at three seconds on any machine.**~~ **IT IS AN OBSERVATION NOW. Corrected 2026-09-09 at Build, step 10a piece E** ([Section 12](#12-unresolved) item 14). **At 51.9 MB/s a three-second window was observed holding about six hundred counts**, on a real transfer through Smoozy's own byte loop over a real SSH connection on the loopback address. **And the argument is what decided the length**: at a slow single channel the floor is R6's own accounting period, a tenth of a second, so a one-second window holds about ten counts and one write moves the figure by a tenth where three seconds bounds it at a thirtieth. The five seconds [R16](#85-what-is-not-observed-and-it-is-named-here-rather-than-in-a-footnote) watched still vindicates the **shape** and no length, exactly as this bullet has always said.
- **The upper bound is [R10](#83-a-cap-reaches-transfers-already-running-which-is-row-306)'s.** A window spanning a cap change *"reads between the two rates rather than at either"*, so **the window's length is exactly how long the figure beside a just-changed cap is wrong** — and the same after a channel is admitted or ends. Three seconds bounds that at three seconds.
- **Both ends of the window are on the one clock the accounting point runs on**, so nothing here subtracts readings taken on two clocks ([State, identity and recovery](State,%20identity%20and%20recovery.md#3-desired-state-and-observed-state) D5). The reading still carries its moment and its clock under [R13](#84-the-achieved-rate-reading).
- **Before a full window has passed, the divisor is the elapsed time and never the window.** Otherwise every transfer's first three seconds read low, and the reading is drawn from the first moment there is one.
- **When it is published, and there is no timer under it.** At each accounting interval, which exists only while a channel is running; **and once more at the moment a direction's last channel ends, where it is published as zero.** A trailing window with nothing left to count is never run down, because running it down would need a timer `Desktop app and delivery` [P10b](Desktop%20app%20and%20delivery.md#33-ui-event-delivery) forbids — so the direction's reading is stated as zero at that moment and stands until a channel is admitted again.
- **The three seconds is one constant in one place.** Moving it changes no shape here and no promise anywhere; it changes only how steady the figure reads and how long R10's mixed reading lasts.

**R13. It is a reading and it carries the moment it was taken and the clock it was taken on** ([State, identity and recovery](State,%20identity%20and%20recovery.md#3-desired-state-and-observed-state) §3, D5). It is **period-held** ([State, identity and recovery](State,%20identity%20and%20recovery.md#111-five-classes-and-the-reason-there-is-no-sixth) §11.1) — every live reading is — so nothing durable is written from it and no promise rests on it.

**R14. Fact 75's read half is answered and the reading is real:** caps were observed holding at 409 KB/s against a requested 400 on concurrent uploads to two boxes, and at 402.8 and 405.5 KB/s on concurrent downloads against 9.44 MB/s uncapped on the identical transfer, so **the rate actually achieved was read.** Where that reading sits on a screen is Design's; that there is one is Row 289's second clause and this is it.

### 8.5 What is not observed, and it is named here rather than in a footnote

**R15. The shared budget was exercised on uploads only, and the download case differs materially in the source's own words:** *"a receiver enforces a cap by not reading, so buffering can let the wire rate overshoot"* (Fact 75). [Section 12](#12-unresolved) item 6 states what breaks. **R2 is unchanged by it** — the enforcement point and the shape are the same in both directions — but the down cap's fidelity to the wire is not established, and the reading of [8.4](#84-the-achieved-rate-reading) is a count of bytes Smoozy accepted rather than of bytes the line carried.

**R16. ANSWERED 2026-08-22, and the answer is a correction rather than a confirmation. The mid-flight change has now been run four times.** This clause used to read *The mid-flight change of R8 was not observed at all … What has never been run is a budget changed while channels are moving.* **It was run in both directions under two different governors** ([The multi-provider batch](The%20multi-provider%20batch%20%E2%80%94%20the%20surface%20that%20did%20not%20vary,%20the%20Debian%20route%20that%20exists,%20and%20the%20cards%20a%20slice%20cannot%20see.md)), and what it exposed is [R6b](#82-the-enforcement-point-is-smoozys-own-byte-loop-and-the-evidence-forced-it): **a cumulative rate governor makes a cap change unsafe in both directions** — a raise burst to about double the new cap for minutes, a lowering fell to about a quarter of it — while a bounded five-second window landed the change inside one minute in both directions. **So the mechanism works and R6 alone did not specify it**, which is why R6b is written as a requirement. **R12a's shape is vindicated by the same run and its length is not.** The bounded trailing window is exactly the shape that behaved; **the length that behaved was five seconds and R12a fixes three**, ~~which was chosen and has still never been watched at any length ([Section 12](#12-unresolved) item 14)~~ **which was watched at Build on 2026-09-09 and stands — [Section 12](#12-unresolved) item 14 carries the figures.** [Section 12](#12-unresolved) item 6's upload half closes with this.

**R17. What the link actually carries is the user's own uplink and not the machine's advertised one, and the asymmetry is about six to one. Added 2026-08-22, measured.** [The multi-provider batch](The%20multi-provider%20batch%20%E2%80%94%20the%20surface%20that%20did%20not%20vary,%20the%20Debian%20route%20that%20exists,%20and%20the%20cards%20a%20slice%20cannot%20see.md), on a rented container in Norway with a **419 430 400**-byte file moved at the link's full speed: **up 0.62 MB/s, down 3.88 MB/s, and 4.31 MB/s on an immediate repeat.** The round-trip digest matched the source exactly. **The seller advertised 580 Mbit up on that host and the observed 0.62 MB/s is about 5 Mbit** — so the constraint was the desktop's uplink throughout. The 4 GiB endurance run showed the same shape on a different box and must not be read as the same measurement: its **upload was deliberately held near 384 KB/s** and so measures nothing about the link, while its **download ran uncapped at 4.03 MB/s**. **The six-to-one figure is the Norway box's alone**; what the endurance run adds is that the download direction reached a comparable rate on other hardware. **So anything that budgets an upload from a seller's advertised figure will be wrong by an order of magnitude, and every duration estimate has to be direction-aware.** **This document supplies the observation and composes no estimate** — a duration shown to a user is `Queue & History`'s and `Download`'s. **And nothing throttled:** the second download was **faster** than the first, so no shaping appeared after several hundred megabytes; a visible drop on the repeat would have been the negative and there was none.

---

## 9. Free space, and the moment the reading is taken

**Constraint:** Row 287, quoted at the head of this document. Reading a machine's free space is `File Browser`'s to promise under Row 184, and this carve-out *"reaches no further than that reading does"*. `Desktop app and delivery` presents it.

### 9.1 A reading is a choice of mount, and the mount is the one the write was attempted on

**F1. Every free-space reading in this document is taken against an exact path, and never against a machine.** Fact 28 is unambiguous: *"the machine's free disk space is not a reading, it is a choice of which mount to read. Reading by total size, or by 'the biggest', or by summing, all pick up something that is not the machine's."* On one container a plain reading returned **51 filesystems**, 48 of them memory-backed *"including one per CPU thermal-throttle folder"*, and of the three that were not, one was **the host's own 32 GiB disk bind-mounted in to deliver the driver files, showing 20 GiB free the rental may not use**.

**F1a. The per-path reading describes the layer the bytes actually land on, and it was honest at the boundary too. Added 2026-08-22, observed.** [The multi-provider batch](The%20multi-provider%20batch%20%E2%80%94%20the%20surface%20that%20did%20not%20vary,%20the%20Debian%20route%20that%20exists,%20and%20the%20cards%20a%20slice%20cannot%20see.md), on a container with a 60 GB allocation on a host holding far more: the subsystem's own reading returned `f_frsize` 4096, `f_blocks` 15 728 640, `f_bavail` 15 728 451 — **64 424 509 440 bytes, exactly the 60 GiB rented**, and not the host's 224 GB disk nor its 32 GB one. The machine's own reading agreed to the byte. The root is `overlay`, its quota enforced by the host's project quota. **And on the machine deliberately filled, the reading read `0` free while the machine's own tool read 100%** — so the figure does not lag or round at the boundary, which is where a free-space reading is worth having at all. **Not posed:** whether a container whose writable layer is **not** quota-backed reports the host's disk. That box's was.

**F1b. On a container some paths are not on the rented layer at all, and the reading names the path for that reason as well. Added 2026-08-22, observed.** On the same box `/etc/hosts`, `/etc/hostname` and `/etc/resolv.conf` were **individually bind-mounted from the host's disk**, and `/usr/bin/nvidia-smi` and every driver library were bind-mounted **read-only** from another of the host's partitions — which is how the driver arrives without the image carrying it. **A write under one of those paths is not on the rented layer**, and a free-space reading taken against one describes the host. **Nothing in this document writes outside the areas the placement made**, so no route here reaches such a path; it is recorded so that no later reader takes an arbitrary path on a container to be the rental's.

**F2. The reading is a two-route negotiation in declared order, like every other capability in [1.2](#12-capability-negotiation-and-it-is-load-bearing-rather-than-a-courtesy), and both routes are per-path.**

1. **The file-transfer subsystem's per-path filesystem-statistics extension, where the connection's announced set carries it.** Observed on the Creator's Mac, macOS 26.5, `OpenSSH_10.2p1`, on 2026-08-17, over a pipe to OpenSSH's own server subsystem: `statvfs@openssh.com` against a named path returned a per-mount reading — fragment size 4,096, and available blocks the same as free blocks on the filesystem tested. Documented — `sftp(1)`, macOS 26.5, dated December 6, 2024, read 2026-08-17: `df` *"Display usage information for the filesystem holding the current directory (or path if specified) … This command is only supported on servers that implement the 'statvfs@openssh.com' extension."*
2. **Otherwise, a per-path reading taken on the machine itself over the command channel, against the exact path — the path itself never in the command string**: after hand-over it rides the entry point's framed standard input, and before hand-over [1.1](#11-what-the-surface-is-made-of-and-it-is-two-mechanisms-rather-than-one)'s planted-link carriage. That is the route by which Fact 28 was answered **on a rental** rather than on the caller's own end: *"**Free space can be read over the connection, by root and by an ordinary login**, both through `df` and through the filesystem call underneath it"* ([Two more boxes](Two%20more%20boxes%20—%20the%20volume,%20the%20full%20disk,%20and%20a%20day%20left%20running.md), Fact 28). It asks the machine about **one named path** and reads the answer for that path alone.

**The four-value reading of [1.1](#11-what-the-surface-is-made-of-and-it-is-two-mechanisms-rather-than-one) rides item 2's route after hand-over and is a different request. Added 2026-10-03, out of step 10c's slow send, ruled by the build runner; no auditor of this document has seen it.** Its paths go on the entry point's standard input as item 2's path does, but it answers four values and no free-space figure. This clause, its two routes and their order do not change.

**Publication and confirmation, 2026-11-06:** `Dependency Upload & Remap` Q5 route 1 gathers publication reads in groups of at most 64 files per upload channel and Q7 gathers every placement of a folder row. Both use §1.1's existing many-path request and its frame bounds; refusal or no answer keeps the command route. An absent answer is checked by the existing subsystem stat where Q7 needs to mark a placement gone. This changes neither the request set nor protocol version. No auditor has seen this change.

**F2 governs the readings this document takes over the connection, and route (b)'s carried reading is not one of them. Added 2026-08-22 with [F6a](#92-the-two-routes-to-the-cause-and-the-moment-each-is-fixed-at).** That one is taken on the machine by Smoozy's own supervisor after hand-over, negotiates nothing, and is unavailable only where the machine did not record it — so neither route here being announced does not withdraw it, and F6a item 6 is what governs its absence.

**The reading is reported unavailable only where both routes are absent.** **And neither route is Fact 28's 51-row trap**, which is a figure *derived from a filesystem list* — picking the biggest, summing, or taking the machine's. A per-path answer against the exact path is not that trap, whichever route produced it; it is F1's requirement obtained the way Fact 28 obtained it. **Deriving a figure from a filesystem list stays forbidden on both routes**, and so does widening a per-path answer into a per-machine one ([F16](#94-what-the-carve-out-may-not-claim)). [Section 12](#12-unresolved) item 3 records what is not established about the second route.

**F3. The available figure is the one read, never the free figure.** Fact 28: *"the root-reserved blocks, which is why the available figure and not the free figure is the one to read."* On one machine `Reserved block count: 0` and the two agreed; that is one box, not a rule.

**One consequence of the reserve is worth stating so nobody later repairs it, recorded 2026-08-21.** **Where a mount has such a reserve** — `mke2fs(8)` says what it is on an ext filesystem, *"the percentage of the file system blocks reserved for the super-user … The default percentage is 5%."* — on a mount exhausted for ordinary users **the available figure reads zero while a write made as the superuser still succeeds**. That looks like a contradiction against `State, identity and recovery` §8.3, which withdraws a disk-full condition only on *"a non-zero free-space reading **and** a real write to that place succeeding — both, not either"*. **It is not one, and the asymmetry runs the safe way:** the reading half is unsatisfied, so the machine stays cautious where a write would have passed. **The *both* rule must not be relaxed to *either* on the strength of a superuser write succeeding.**

**The 5% is an ext default and is not a reading of any machine this project has met — scoped 2026-08-23; this note read as a statement about rented machines.** A container rental's root is `overlay` over the host's project quota ([S13](#74-resume-never-trusts-a-partial-it-has-not-verified), [F1a](#91-a-reading-is-a-choice-of-mount-and-the-mount-is-the-one-the-write-was-attempted-on)), which `mke2fs(8)` does not speak for at all, and Fact 28's own box read **`Reserved block count: 0`** with the available and free figures agreeing. **The rule above holds without the number**: F3 reads the available figure whatever the reserve is, including zero, and the *both, not either* rule is what stops a superuser write from withdrawing a condition — neither depends on any mount actually carrying a reserve, and the note exists only to say what a non-zero one would do.

**F4. Free space is never computed by subtracting listed file sizes.** Fact 28's fifth exclusion: a 500 GB sparse file *"sat in the measured folder and moved the free-space reading by nothing."* Fact 7 states the same trap from the listing side — *"size can be a lie … Any size shown to a user, any will-it-fit check and any transfer estimate must decide which number it means."* **Every size this document reports says which of the two it is.**

**F5. A writability test is not a reading and is not a signal.** Observed: on a full filesystem *"the writability test **returns writable**, and **creating an empty file succeeds**"* — an empty inode needs no data blocks — and *"Only writing at least one byte fails"* (Fact 28). [State, identity and recovery](State,%20identity%20and%20recovery.md#13-rules-that-bind-every-store) §1.3 rule 4 already binds placement to a real write of at least one byte, and the same rule binds this document.

### 9.2 The two routes to the cause, and the moment each is fixed at

**F6. The row's undecided moment — *"Nothing anywhere says at what moment the reading is taken or how far it may be trusted afterwards"* — is settled by fixing a moment for each of two routes.** The carve-out fires where **either**:

- **(a) Smoozy's own write to that place was refused with the out-of-space condition** (F7). The cause is established at the refusal, which happened at the moment that matters, and the reading only carries the amount.
- **(b) a render ended failing, and a free-space reading taken against that job's own output path reports nothing available** — the reading taken **on the machine, in the act that writes that render's recorded outcome**, and carried with that outcome to whoever reads it ([F6a](#92-the-two-routes-to-the-cause-and-the-moment-each-is-fixed-at)). That is the moment the row leaves open, and pinning it there is what makes the reading a statement about the place as it was when the render ended rather than about the place at whatever later minute a desktop happened to connect.

  **Corrected 2026-08-22, scenario replays Pile 1 item R11.** This used to pin route (b) to *"the same request round in which the ending is learned, and before anything else runs on that connection"* — the moment the **desktop** learns the ending, which is not a moment on the machine at all and gave route (b) no establish-at-the-moment discipline of any kind. With auto-download off and Smoozy closed, Smoozy makes no write of its own, so route (a) cannot fire and route (b) is the whole of the carve-out; a user who frees the disk before the desktop next connects then gets a roomy reading and no diagnosis, in precisely the case [Whole app](../Specs/Whole%20app.md) wrote the carve-out for. **Freeing the space was what silenced it, and that is the one thing an establish-at-the-moment rule exists to stop.**

**Route (b) exists because route (a) alone cannot fire in the case the spec puts inside the promise.** Where the disk fills under a render's own output, the write refused was Blender's and not Smoozy's (F9), so no refusal of Smoozy's exists to read a cause off; and [Whole app](../Specs/Whole%20app.md) is explicit that this case is covered — *"A job with a frame still to come ends at a **failing** status, and everything above fires as written, the disk-full carve-out included."* **Neither route replaces the other**: route (a) is the only one that closes both of the row's named harms outright, and route (b) is the only one that reaches the row's own subject.

**F6a. What route (b)'s reading is, what carries it, and what happens where it is absent. Added 2026-08-22, scenario replays Pile 1 item R11.**

1. **It is taken on the machine, in the same act that writes the render's recorded outcome** — the ordinary ending the supervisor takes from the wait status, and equally the ending a reconciliation writes for a group that is gone ([State, identity and recovery](State,%20identity%20and%20recovery.md#51-the-durable-operation-record) §5.1, [§5.3](State,%20identity%20and%20recovery.md#53-the-deliberate-stop) step 6; `Rented-server runtime` M17). Whichever act wrote the outcome is the reading's moment, and the reading carries that moment on the machine's own clock ([F10](#93-what-the-reading-carries-and-how-far-it-may-be-trusted), [State, identity and recovery](State,%20identity%20and%20recovery.md#3-desired-state-and-observed-state) D5).
2. **It is [F1](#91-a-reading-is-a-choice-of-mount-and-the-mount-is-the-one-the-write-was-attempted-on)'s per-path reading against that job's own output path and nothing wider** — [F3](#91-a-reading-is-a-choice-of-mount-and-the-mount-is-the-one-the-write-was-attempted-on)'s available figure, never [F4](#91-a-reading-is-a-choice-of-mount-and-the-mount-is-the-one-the-write-was-attempted-on)'s subtraction, never [F5](#91-a-reading-is-a-choice-of-mount-and-the-mount-is-the-one-the-write-was-attempted-on)'s writability test, and never widened into a statement about the machine ([F16](#94-what-the-carve-out-may-not-claim)).
3. **It needs no announced extension and no program on the machine.** After hand-over the supervisor is Smoozy's own code and reads the filesystem call directly — the route Fact 28 answered on a rental, *"both through `df` and through the filesystem call underneath it"*, and the one [F1a](#91-a-reading-is-a-choice-of-mount-and-the-mount-is-the-one-the-write-was-attempted-on) observed reading `0` on a machine deliberately filled while the machine's own tool read 100%. **So route (b) does not stand on [Section 12](#12-unresolved) item 3's which-program ground**, which [F2](#91-a-reading-is-a-choice-of-mount-and-the-mount-is-the-one-the-write-was-attempted-on)'s second route does. `Rented-server runtime` V7 already names [9.1](#91-a-reading-is-a-choice-of-mount-and-the-mount-is-the-one-the-write-was-attempted-on) as owning the mechanism it uses.
4. **It rides the write that records the outcome, so it needs no durability the ending does not already need**, and it reaches a reader by the same route the ending does ([G4](#102-what-each-kind-of-work-has-to-report-and-from-where)). **On a mount the render has filled, that write is refused and both it and this reading ride the pending outcome under `Rented-server runtime` S14** — the ending is late rather than guaranteed. **M4a's control-state reserve is not what leaves room for it**: M4a claims no protection from the user's outputs or another process filling that mount, and `Job files and outputs` L4a and its §14 item 21 say the separation is placement rather than protection. **Corrected 2026-09-01**, scenario replay *The server disk fills up during render* finding N-d, in the same act as `Rented-server runtime` V12's *What it rides*, which carried the same wrong sentence.
5. **No reading taken at the moment the desktop learns the ending feeds route (b)**, and a builder that keeps both has kept the fault this clause closes.
6. **Where a recorded outcome carries no such reading, route (b) does not fire.** The machine's own words stand alone, which is already what [F17](#94-what-the-carve-out-may-not-claim) says the carve-out does with no reading available. Nothing is re-read to supply one, and nothing infers a full disk from the reading's absence.

**The machine-side act is `Rented-server runtime`'s to state and is deliberately not written here.** **Two lines were owed to that document and both were carried on 2026-08-22 — corrected 2026-08-29, this having gone on recording them as owed a week after they landed, which sends a reader looking for work that is done.** Its **V12** takes this reading in the same act that writes the recorded outcome and carries it with that outcome and its moment; and its **V5**, which read *"Nothing durable is written from one … no reading is ever journalled or written into a job record"*, now reads *"that half holds for the readings this section is about"* — the narrowing, because a reading recorded as part of an ending is not a live value with a lifetime but a fact about a past moment that cannot stop being true ([State, identity and recovery](State,%20identity%20and%20recovery.md#81-two-kinds-and-the-kind-is-declared) §8.1's *ending* kind). **That document already held one such recording** — M4a's capture ceiling, taken through V7's free-space route and *"recorded by value before the worker starts"* — so the narrowing was owed whether or not this clause landed. **The one line owed to `Desktop app and delivery`** [16.4](Desktop%20app%20and%20delivery.md#164-row-287-presented-the-disk-full-carve-out) **is carried at M15a**: route (b)'s reading arrives carried with the ending rather than taken when the ending is learned, and M15's *"as of the moment it was taken … never re-read to keep a message fresh"* is what already governs it.

**F7. The refusal is a usable signal and it was observed to be uniform.** Fact 28: every write on a full filesystem was refused with **`No space left on device`**, and a Python write with an explicit flush and `fsync` raised **errno 28** — from a shell redirect, from `cp`, and from that flushed write alike. *"A full disk refuses in one way, everywhere."* **So a request whose failure carries that condition is route (a); a request whose failure carries anything else is not**, and no reading may promote it into one.

**F7a. A full disk is also logged by the machine, and damage in the pipe is not — the two are not equally silent, and nothing may be built on their being so. Added 2026-08-22.** [The multi-provider batch](The%20multi-provider%20batch%20%E2%80%94%20the%20surface%20that%20did%20not%20vary,%20the%20Debian%20route%20that%20exists,%20and%20the%20cards%20a%20slice%20cannot%20see.md): at the moment the filesystem filled, the machine's own log carried `sftp-server[1053]: error: process_write: write "…route1.bin.smoozy-diskfull.part": No space left on device`, and the same line for the second route. **Fact 72's corruption case produced nothing at all.** So a builder who reasons *the machine said nothing, therefore it was not the disk* is right, and one who reasons *the machine says nothing about either, so the log is useless* is wrong. **Nothing here reads the machine's log** — it is not on the surface [1.1](#11-what-the-surface-is-made-of-and-it-is-two-mechanisms-rather-than-one) admits — and the asymmetry is recorded beside [S21](#76-integrity-what-is-compared-and-when) and this clause so that the silence of the corruption case is never generalised.

**F8. Three consequences, and the first two are the row's two named harms closed on both routes. Widened from route (a) alone on 2026-08-22, scenario replays Pile 1 item R11.**

- **A disk freed between the failure and the reading cannot make the failure read as roomy**, because on **both** routes the cause is established at the moment it happened — on route (a) at the refusal, on route (b) at the act that writes the recorded outcome ([F6a](#92-the-two-routes-to-the-cause-and-the-moment-each-is-fixed-at)). A later roomy reading changes the **amount** a fresh reading would report, not whether the place was full then. **This bullet used to protect route (a) and only route (a)**, and route (b) had no such discipline at all.
- **A disk filled afterwards by something else cannot be attached to a failure that had another cause**, because a failure that did not carry the out-of-space condition is not route (a) at all, whatever any later reading says. **On route (b) the same harm is bounded rather than closed**, and [F9](#92-the-two-routes-to-the-cause-and-the-moment-each-is-fixed-at) states exactly what is left of it.
- **The reading and the place must be the same place, on both routes.** On route (a) the reading is taken against the exact path the write was attempted on (F1), in the same request round as the refusal, before anything else runs on that connection. On route (b) it is taken against that job's own output path, on the machine, in the act that writes that render's recorded outcome.

**F9. Nothing here diagnoses a render failure by reading a machine for a cause the machine did not state.** The carve-out's own subject is a render that failed, and Row 286's raw record is `Rented-server runtime`'s. **The render's own writes are Blender's, not Smoozy's**, so route (a)'s out-of-space condition — which comes from **Smoozy's own write to that place** — does not exist in that case, and what such a run prints about the disk is labelled unresolved in the owning spec and no row is filed on it. **That is why route (b) reads the place rather than the run**: it says the place this job wrote to had no room at the moment that job's ending was recorded, and it never converts that into a statement about why the render ended (F14). **What is left on route (b) is a residual the row's own words grant, and it is narrower than it was**: a mount that something else had filled by the moment this render's outcome was recorded is reported as having no room, which is true of the place and is said alongside the machine's own words rather than as a cause. **What is no longer inside it is everything after that moment** — the whole stretch between the render ending and a desktop learning of it, which is the long one, and which the reading used to be taken at the far end of ([F6](#92-the-two-routes-to-the-cause-and-the-moment-each-is-fixed-at), corrected 2026-08-22).

### 9.3 What the reading carries, and how far it may be trusted

**F10. The reading is presented as of the moment it was taken, on the machine's own clock** ([State, identity and recovery](State,%20identity%20and%20recovery.md#3-desired-state-and-observed-state) §3, D5), and it is never re-read to keep a message fresh. [State, identity and recovery](State,%20identity%20and%20recovery.md#84-nothing-is-polled-to-withdraw-a-record) §8.4 forbids polling and names the three moments a condition's observed value is refreshed; none of them is a watcher of this document's.

**F11. The disk-full condition is raised, keyed and withdrawn by [State, identity and recovery](State,%20identity%20and%20recovery.md#83-the-condition-identity-and-who-supplies-each-observed-value) §8.3 and this document supplies its two observed values.** That table already fixes the shape: the condition key carries **the location it was read against, in one normalised form**, so a disk-full condition naming *the machine* would fold two conditions into one record; and withdrawal takes **both** signals — a non-zero free-space reading **and** a real write to that place succeeding. Both were observed available: deleting the large file *"returned the free space at once … and a copy into the same folder that had just refused succeeded"* (Fact 28). **What this document supplies to that condition is the free-space reading of F2 and the outcome of the next real write to that place**, and nothing else — route (b)'s carried reading is supplied to the render's recorded ending instead, and [F11a](#93-what-the-reading-carries-and-how-far-it-may-be-trusted) says why the two may not be swapped.

**Which of the two the location is, said once so a builder does not have to choose.** The reading and the refusal are taken against the **exact path** (F1, F8, unchanged). The condition **key** carries the **mount** that path resolves to, in one normalised form, which is what [State, identity and recovery](State,%20identity%20and%20recovery.md#83-the-condition-identity-and-who-supplies-each-observed-value) §8.3's own kind — *disk full at a mount* — already names and what its §8.9 means by *"A hundred files refused by one full mount on one machine are one condition on one subject."* On the path reading, a pool of channels refused on one full mount would raise one condition identity per path and Row 288's *said once* would be unimplementable at the only layer that can implement it. **What resolves a path to a mount is F2's second route** — a per-path reading taken on the machine over the command channel names the filesystem the path sits on, which the subsystem extension's figures do not. That route needs no announced extension, so the resolution is available on every connection the command channel is; [Section 12](#12-unresolved) item 3 records what is not established about it.

**F11a. Route (b)'s carried reading is an observation on the render's recorded ending, and it is never a supplier of the live disk-full condition. Added 2026-08-22, scenario replays Pile 1 item R11.** The two are different records with different lifetimes ([State, identity and recovery](State,%20identity%20and%20recovery.md#81-two-kinds-and-the-kind-is-declared) §8.1), and [Whole app](../Specs/Whole%20app.md) draws the line itself in the clause that ends a failure message: *"**That covers a message about a condition that is still live, and never a job's recorded ending.** A failed job stays failed however the machine's state changes afterwards: freeing the disk does not put the frames back that were never rendered, so the reason for keeping the record has not been served at all."* Three things follow.

- **The live condition keeps §8.3's own two suppliers, both of them current** — the mount's free-space reading and the next real write to that place — and a carried reading is neither. [§8.2](State,%20identity%20and%20recovery.md#82-a-condition-record-is-a-derivation-never-a-copy) presents a condition record *"while, and only while, the condition's current observed value is one of the values it was raised over"*, so raising a live condition from a reading about a past moment would put up a record the next refresh withdraws — the app being wrong on screen about its own state.
- **The carried reading belongs to the render's recorded ending**, is immutable with it ([§8.6](State,%20identity%20and%20recovery.md#86-the-subject-outcome-is-durable-and-immutable)), and is not withdrawn by the disk being freed. That is the spec's own sentence above, kept rather than restated.
- **Where the mount is still full when something next reads it, the live condition is raised by its own suppliers as usual**, against its own durable subject ([§8.7](State,%20identity%20and%20recovery.md#87-every-failure-record-names-a-durable-subject)). Neither record is derived from the other and neither is composed out of both; what is shown, and in what order, is `Desktop app and delivery`'s.

**F12. A refusal for any other reason is a different condition with a different supplier**, per the same table's separate row, and a fresher free-space reading is not a supplier for it. That split is [State, identity and recovery](State,%20identity%20and%20recovery.md#83-the-condition-identity-and-who-supplies-each-observed-value) §8.3's and is cited rather than re-argued.

**F13. Two failures on one full mount are one condition, which is what makes Row 288's *said once* implementable.** The repeat key is the condition identity ([State, identity and recovery](State,%20identity%20and%20recovery.md#89-the-repeat-key-supplied-rather-than-applied) §8.9), and *"A hundred files refused by one full mount on one machine are one condition on one subject."* **Whether and how it is used to say one thing once is `Desktop app and delivery`'s under Row 288** and nothing here answers it; what is supplied is that a pool of channels failing at one moment for one reason resolves to one condition key because F11 normalises the location.

### 9.4 What the carve-out may not claim

**F14. It never says a render failed because the disk was full.** It says what a reading against a place says, and no more — on route (a), that the place a write was refused for space is out of space; on route (b), that the place this job wrote to had no room at the moment that job's ending was recorded ([F6a](#92-the-two-routes-to-the-cause-and-the-moment-each-is-fixed-at), the second half added 2026-08-22 with the moment it names). It says so **alongside the machine's own words rather than instead of them**, which is the row's own wording. Row 286's raw record is unedited and `Rented-server runtime`'s.

**F15. It never claims the reading is current.** F10.

**F16. It never claims the amount the reading gives is the amount the rental may use.** The container case is why: the host's disk bind-mounted in showed 20 GiB free the rental may not touch (Fact 28). F1 and F2 close it for a reading taken against the right path, and F16 is stated so nothing downstream widens a per-path answer into a per-machine one.

**F17. Where neither free-space route is available, a refusal Smoozy's own write met still names its cause and carries no amount — and that is the spec's general rule rather than Row 287's carve-out.** The behaviour stands and its grant is [Whole app](../Specs/Whole%20app.md)'s general rule: *"**It says what to do only where Smoozy knows.** Where the cause is genuinely known — the folder files are landing in is full … Smoozy says the thing to do about it."* A write refused with the out-of-space condition (F7) is genuinely known, and that rule names the full folder as its own example. **Row 287 is the narrower carve-out for a *render* failure**, and its ceiling clause *"this reaches no further than that reading does"* caps the carve-out rather than the general rule. **So this clause is filed against the general rule and not against Row 287** — which also settles what the carve-out itself does with no reading available: route (b) does not exist without one, and route (a)'s message, cause and no figure, is the general rule speaking. What the user is shown is `Desktop app and delivery`'s and Design's.

---

## 10. Nothing is carried out with nothing to report until it is done

**Constraint:** Row 291, quoted at the head of this document. Drawing the indicator is Build's, where it sits is Design's, and `Desktop app and delivery` presents it. **What is owed here is that no piece of work in this document's scope is carried out in a way that has nothing to report until it is done.**

### 10.1 The rule, put as a requirement on every request this document defines

**G1. Every request has at least one report before its result.** Concretely, and this is the floor rather than a wish: a request is **recorded locally before it is sent** (C3), it is **acknowledged as recorded before its work completes** (C7), and its work's own progress reaches the caller by one of the three routes in [10.2](#102-what-each-kind-of-work-has-to-report-and-from-where). **A request with none of the three is a fault in this section rather than a case it does not cover.**

**G2. C7 is what makes G1 more than a restatement.** Because an acknowledgement says the request is recorded and never that the work is done, *it has started* and *it has finished* are two separate correlated answers rather than one. A protocol in which the only answer is the finished one is a protocol in which every noticeable moment is silent, and Row 291 is what forbids designing that.

**G3. Nothing waits silently for a state transition.** Every waiter has its own deadline and **expiry is reported** (C17). So the longest a caller can go with nothing to say is its own deadline, and that is a reportable event rather than a hang.

### 10.2 What each kind of work has to report, and from where

**G4. Three routes, and each kind of work in this document's scope uses one of them.**

| Kind of work | What it reports, and from where |
|---|---|
| A **transfer** | bytes staged against bytes expected, counted at the byte loop's own accounting interval ([8.2](#82-the-enforcement-point-is-smoozys-own-byte-loop-and-the-evidence-forced-it)) and never derived from asking the far end for a file's size. The interval is bounded above by R6's fixed short period, in time and not only in bytes, so the gap between reports is bounded. |
| A **request the machine took as an operation** | the event journal ([State, identity and recovery](State,%20identity%20and%20recovery.md#4-the-event-journal-and-acknowledgement) §4), read forward from the caller's own durable cursor. Reconciliation dispositions are journalled every time they are written — *"an attempt a reader never heard of is the case this step exists for"* (§6 step 4) — so a resumed or re-run attempt is reportable rather than invisible. |
| A **reading** — a listing, an attribute read, a free-space reading, a hardware reading, package inspection, a digest over a byte range | its own result, and the fact that it was submitted. A reading that cannot report progress inside itself is bounded by its waiter's deadline instead (G3). |

**G4a. A transfer that starts over reports the start-over, and the count falling to zero is never left to stand as the whole report. Added 2026-08-21, per Row 415.** [G4](#102-what-each-kind-of-work-has-to-report-and-from-where)'s count is bytes staged against bytes expected, and two clauses of [Section 7](#7-staging-integrity-resume-and-publication--one-contract-in-both-directions) discard the staged material and begin again from zero **while the transfer keeps its identity and is neither failed nor ended** — [S12](#74-resume-never-trusts-a-partial-it-has-not-verified) step 5, the staged prefix not verifying, and [S13](#74-resume-never-trusts-a-partial-it-has-not-verified), a **download's** source having changed under the transfer. **Narrowed to the download direction 2026-08-31**: on the upload side a source that has *changed* no longer starts over at all — it stops and is reported, which is [S13](#74-resume-never-trusts-a-partial-it-has-not-verified)'s corrected supply and [`File Browser`](File%20Browser.md) K10 clause 1's — so what remains of S13 on that side as a start-over cause is a source merely unconfirmed, taken through S12 step 5. **So the count truthfully goes back to nothing on a transfer nothing has ended.** **[S24](#76-integrity-what-is-compared-and-when) is not one of them and is named so it is not swept in**: a digest disagreeing at the end **ends the transfer as *failed*** ([S26](#78-cleanup-and-the-five-ways-a-transfer-ends)), and *"trying again is a new transfer with a new identity"* — a fresh count from zero there belongs to a different transfer and needs nothing added.

- **The count is the truth and is never held up.** Bytes staged is bytes staged: on a fresh staging path the report is zero and climbs. Nothing carries the abandoned run's figure forward and nothing smooths the fall, which is [G5](#102-what-each-kind-of-work-has-to-report-and-from-where)'s discipline applied to Smoozy's own earlier count instead of to the far end's.
- **What is added is one field beside the count and never a second figure: the transfer's report carries that it started over, and which of the two causes it was.** The mark is set when the fresh staging path is opened and stands until that transfer ends under [S26](#78-cleanup-and-the-five-ways-a-transfer-ends). **It rides with the report and nothing durable is written from it** — it is period-held like the count, and it needs no lifetime beyond the run, because a start-over is a step inside one run and no record from an earlier run is ever resumed automatically ([C11](#44-re-establishing-after-a-connection-servers-replaced) step 4's run marker).
- **The mark rather than an inference, and the reason is that no reader can recover it.** From the count alone a transfer that has just begun, one that has begun again, and one whose first chunk is still in flight are the same report. **Nothing infers a start-over from the count falling**, exactly as nothing infers an ending from a rate falling.
- **It also keeps [G7](#103-a-slow-thing-and-a-stopped-one)'s stall test honest.** That test reads the last progress moment; a start-over moves it, so without the mark a restart is indistinguishable from progress and a channel that keeps restarting reads as a channel that keeps moving.
- **A start-over is none of S26's five endings and this changes that in no way** (S13). The transfer's identity, its record and its request are untouched, and what is reported is a step inside one transfer rather than an outcome.
- **The cost is declared rather than glossed:** the whole of the abandoned prefix crosses twice, bounded by the unit's own size. [S11](#74-resume-never-trusts-a-partial-it-has-not-verified) is why it is paid — the standard tool's own resume is unverified by construction and *"the resultant file is likely to be corrupt."*

**G4b. A transfer verifying a staged prefix on a resume reports that it is verifying, and the unmoving count is never the whole report. Added 2026-08-23, per [7.4](#74-resume-never-trusts-a-partial-it-has-not-verified).** [S12](#74-resume-never-trusts-a-partial-it-has-not-verified) step 3's verification moves no bytes and does not move the recorded offset, and on a nearly complete 4 GiB prefix it runs **about twelve minutes** at the measured 5.9 MB/s read-back rate. **One field beside the count says the transfer is verifying and against what prefix length**, set when the verification begins and cleared when the first byte above the checkpoint is written; it rides with the report and nothing durable is written from it, exactly as [G4a](#102-what-each-kind-of-work-has-to-report-and-from-where)'s mark does. **[G7](#103-a-slow-thing-and-a-stopped-one)'s stall test does not read a verifying channel as stalled**, and no completion estimate treats a resume as starting at the checkpoint. **The verification draws no rate budget**, so [C19](#46-per-waiter-deadlines)'s inactivity deadline does not fire on it either — that deadline runs only while a channel holds an allowance it cannot spend.

**G5. Progress is never derived from the far end's own view of the file.** For a transfer the count is Smoozy's, at Smoozy's own loop. That is the same reason S10 gives: a staged file's size is not a statement of progress, and a zero-byte one is *"indistinguishable between 'still being written', 'the disk filled' and 'the render wrote nothing'"* (Fact 28).

**G6. A batch of transfers reports both levels.** The per-transfer count of G4 and the enumeration of S9 together give the detail and the whole, which is the primitive Rows 190 and 299 are written against. **What the whole is counted in — files or bytes — and what it reads while a deep folder is still being listed are Rows 299's and 190's**, not settled here; what is supplied is that the enumeration is durable before the first transfer starts (S8), so a whole exists to be read from the first moment.

### 10.3 A slow thing and a stopped one

**G7. Row 291 promises *underway* and not a limit on how long *underway* may last, and this document does not promise to detect a hang.** What it does not do is make one undetectable later: because every transfer's progress is its own byte count with a bounded reporting interval, and every waiter has a deadline, a stalled channel is distinguishable from a moving one by its last progress moment. **An inactivity deadline (C19) is what is built on that**, and its threshold sits well above the longest legitimate stall observed — a frozen transfer at 17,233,920 bytes for 11.1 seconds while unfinished (Fact 20).

**G8. A stalled channel is not a broken connection and neither is inferred from the other.** Fact 57 is the reason a channel's silence cannot stand in for a connection's: a caller's own line pulled went undetected for **21 minutes 45 seconds** with keepalives off, while a machine closing politely and a provider's own stop were both announced in the same second. The connection's observed value comes from `Servers` under [State, identity and recovery](State,%20identity%20and%20recovery.md#85-the-connection-condition-needs-a-break-reported-without-using-the-connection) §8.5; a stalled channel is reported as a stalled channel.

**G9. A waiter's expired deadline never changes what is reported about the work.** C17. A waiter that gave up says the waiter gave up, and the transfer or operation carries on being whatever the record and the journal say it is.

---

## 11. What this document supplies, and to which document

**To `Rented-server runtime` — the request side of everything it answers.** The two mechanisms of the pre-package surface ([1.1](#11-what-the-surface-is-made-of-and-it-is-two-mechanisms-rather-than-one)); the framing, compatibility interval and retained-adapter control routes of [Section 2](#2-request-framing-and-versioning), and the request-set declaration of [1.2](#12-capability-negotiation-and-it-is-load-bearing-rather-than-a-courtesy) item 3; the authentication boundary as the request side of [Security and licence](Security%20and%20licence.md#8-the-supervisors-machine-local-endpoint) §8; the three closed answers of C8; the bootstrap-and-repair request's exact carriage and idempotency against the package-phase record ([B1](#5-the-bootstrap-and-repair-request-and-package-inspection), [B3](#5-the-bootstrap-and-repair-request-and-package-inspection)); **the signed-manifest release descriptor, exact platform/CPU/ABI selection, verification-before-execution and key transition/revocation contract of B8–B10, whose evidence the package-phase record must retain and validate — with B10's at-rest comparison performed by the desktop's subsystem read-back wherever no verified runtime is alive, there being no pre-package wrapper and no Smoozy executable of any kind before hand-over**; package inspection comparing content and never presence ([B4](#5-the-bootstrap-and-repair-request-and-package-inspection)); the staging-and-publication contract of [Section 7](#7-staging-integrity-resume-and-publication--one-contract-in-both-directions); transfer-support termination as staging and cleanup rather than as a signal contract ([C22](#47-cancellation-and-there-are-two-of-them)); **the transfer-support kind's five A1 declarations, added 2026-08-29 at the stage-close gate repair because its W1 names this document their owner and this document had made none, leaving A3's default to arbitrate the kind** ([T11](#64-the-transfer-support-workers-declarations-which-are-this-documents-and-had-not-been-made)); **and the requirement that a render's recorded outcome carry a free-space reading taken against that job's own output path in the act that writes it, with that act's moment on the machine's own clock — route (b)'s whole discipline, whose machine-side act is that document's to state and is named there rather than written here** ([F6a](#92-the-two-routes-to-the-cause-and-the-moment-each-is-fixed-at), added 2026-08-22 for Row 287).

**To `Desktop app and delivery`.** The per-channel enforcement it tells this document to apply, and what the enforcement point has to be for its one shared budget to hold ([Section 8](#8-per-channel-rate-enforcement)); **that the budget's one unit is bytes per second** ([R11](#83-a-cap-reaches-transfers-already-running-which-is-row-306)); **that every admitted channel's draw from that budget is bounded below by a non-zero share**, so *waiting on the budget* is a bounded wait rather than an open one and [C19](#46-per-waiter-deadlines)'s inactivity deadline has something to sit against — a constraint on how the budget is divided, and not a decision taken for its holder; the requirement that admission be one desktop-side point beside that budget ([T5](#62-concurrency-and-one-admission-point-rather-than-one-per-server)); the achieved-rate reading and its class ([8.4](#84-the-achieved-rate-reading)), **including the window it is averaged over, the elapsed-time divisor before that window has filled, and the two moments it is published at — every accounting interval, and once as zero when a direction's last channel ends** ([R12a](#84-the-achieved-rate-reading), added 2026-08-21 for Row 329); the platform-file publication contract's four requirements ([S18](#75-publication-is-the-last-step-and-it-is-one-step)) — the fourth, supplying the stable file identity, added 2026-08-22 with [Section 12](#12-unresolved) item 15 — and what the Mac's own manual says about the operation family ([S19](#75-publication-is-the-last-step-and-it-is-one-step)); the free-space reading and the next-write outcome as the disk-full condition's two observed values ([F11](#93-what-the-reading-carries-and-how-far-it-may-be-trusted)), for presenting Row 287 — **and, added 2026-08-22, that the carve-out's route (b) reading arrives carried with the render's recorded ending rather than taken when the ending is learned, is an observation on that ending and never a supplier of the live condition, and is presented as of its own moment and never re-read** ([F6a](#92-the-two-routes-to-the-cause-and-the-moment-each-is-fixed-at), [F11a](#93-what-the-reading-carries-and-how-far-it-may-be-trusted)); and the reporting floor of [Section 10](#10-nothing-is-carried-out-with-nothing-to-report-until-it-is-done), for presenting Row 291 — **including that a transfer's report carries a start-over mark beside the count, so a progress figure returning to zero is never the whole report** ([G4a](#102-what-each-kind-of-work-has-to-report-and-from-where), added 2026-08-21 for Row 415), **and that a resuming transfer carries a verifying mark beside its count, so a count that does not move is never presented as a stalled transfer, and a completion estimate is not composed over a verifying transfer as though it started at the checkpoint** ([G4b](#102-what-each-kind-of-work-has-to-report-and-from-where), added 2026-08-23). **The automatic supervisor-unavailable repair trigger is that document's; the request it sends is [Section 5](#5-the-bootstrap-and-repair-request-and-package-inspection)'s. And, added 2026-08-29, the verdict on when an unanswered command-path request moves that condition's observed value at all — a request left unanswered inside a break `Servers` then declares moves nothing** ([C17a](#46-per-waiter-deadlines)); the one-line citation owed in its §12 is [Section 12](#12-unresolved) item 22's.

**To `Servers`.** The requirement that the primary connection alternates its one session between reading and command work at a learned cap of one, while any additional connection is transfer-only ([T2](#61-a-transfer-is-a-channel-and-the-channel-is-independent-of-the-command-channel), [T5](#62-concurrency-and-one-admission-point-rather-than-one-per-server)). That a replaced connection replaces channels and nothing else ([C11](#44-re-establishing-after-a-connection-servers-replaced)). That the offered host key is obtainable and comparable before any connection that presents Smoozy's credential ([A5](#3-the-authentication-boundary)), and that **nothing in this document is keyed on it**. That no health check here pings ([C18](#46-per-waiter-deadlines)).

**To `Server Setup`.** The bootstrap-and-repair request and package inspection as the requests its user-facing fallback re-run sends ([Section 5](#5-the-bootstrap-and-repair-request-and-package-inspection)); the pre-package surface's two mechanisms, available before anything is installed ([1.1](#11-what-the-surface-is-made-of-and-it-is-two-mechanisms-rather-than-one)); **the admissibility rule every setup command is checked against, in both phases (corrected 2026-08-31), and the planted-link carriage for the elevated steps that must name a machine-origin path — the hand-over of a chosen root is the case — with the consuming command's link-treatment citation as that document's owed duty** ([1.1](#11-what-the-surface-is-made-of-and-it-is-two-mechanisms-rather-than-one)); the transfer contract for placing material, including that the elevated-access password crosses on an elevating command's standard input and never as an argument ([1.3](#13-what-the-surface-does-not-carry)); and the deferred-upgrade result ([B6](#5-the-bootstrap-and-repair-request-and-package-inspection)).

**To `File Browser` and `Dependency Upload & Remap` — the upload primitives.** The whole of [Section 7](#7-staging-integrity-resume-and-publication--one-contract-in-both-directions), which is what Rows 197, 199 and 200 are written against; that a transfer is bound to its server and not to the browsing position ([T3](#61-a-transfer-is-a-channel-and-the-channel-is-independent-of-the-command-channel)), which is Row 185; the per-server admission point and the measured two-to-four range for Row 188 ([T5](#62-concurrency-and-one-admission-point-rather-than-one-per-server), [T6](#62-concurrency-and-one-admission-point-rather-than-one-per-server)); the priority mechanism for Row 189, whose order those documents declare ([T8](#63-priority-and-what-is-owned-here-is-the-mechanism) to [T10](#63-priority-and-what-is-owned-here-is-the-mechanism)); the five endings, their opposite cleanup rules and which single one of them means the work may be carried on, for Row 197 ([S26](#78-cleanup-and-the-five-ways-a-transfer-ends)); the checkpointed resume for Row 200 ([S12](#74-resume-never-trusts-a-partial-it-has-not-verified)); the requirement that a resume of an upload wait on that module's own unchanged-file answer ([S13](#74-resume-never-trusts-a-partial-it-has-not-verified)), which is Row 201; **and the start-over mark those two cases carry beside the byte count** ([G4a](#102-what-each-kind-of-work-has-to-report-and-from-where), added 2026-08-21 for Row 415). **Listing, attributes and the free-space reading are readings on the surface** ([1.1](#11-what-the-surface-is-made-of-and-it-is-two-mechanisms-rather-than-one), [F2](#91-a-reading-is-a-choice-of-mount-and-the-mount-is-the-one-the-write-was-attempted-on)); **the admissibility rule and planted-link carriage its elevated retries fall under, in both phases** ([1.1](#11-what-the-surface-is-made-of-and-it-is-two-mechanisms-rather-than-one)); and **how far the three listing failures can be separated at all** — the subsystem's own status separates them at readdir **and at stat alike**, returning `SSH_FX_PERMISSION_DENIED 3` for one and `SSH_FX_NO_SUCH_FILE 2` for the other, while an exit code on the command channel separates nothing ([1.3](#13-what-the-surface-does-not-carry)). **Corrected 2026-08-22: this supply line still said that at stat *may not read it* and *gone* are one answer on either route, which §1.3 in this document stopped saying on 2026-08-20.** What is supplied is a separation rather than a limit; the collapse belongs to the transfer client program expanding the path before it asks, not to the protocol. **The codes and their meanings are documented and a real server returning them is observed, on the caller's own end and on no rental** — [1.3](#13-what-the-surface-does-not-carry) carries both labels and [Section 12](#12-unresolved) item 24 carries what is left open. **Added 2026-08-31, at the first audited reading of this document against `File Browser`:** the requirement that a module abandoning an *interrupted* transfer's staging first writes that record to *stopped: abandoned by its request*, or the sweep never reaches the material ([S28a](#78-cleanup-and-the-five-ways-a-transfer-ends)); and that *interrupted* is the only ending meaning the work may be carried on, so no composed presentation may show any other ending as waiting for the connection ([S26b](#78-cleanup-and-the-five-ways-a-transfer-ends)). **Added 2026-09-04 at the code round:** the caller-declared publication gate ([S15b](#75-publication-is-the-last-step-and-it-is-one-step)) — one named moment inside the byte loop, after the pass-through digest and before [S15](#75-publication-is-the-last-step-and-it-is-one-step)'s pre-state, whose refusal takes [S26](#78-cleanup-and-the-five-ways-a-transfer-ends)'s *stopped: the source changed under it* row — which is the moment `File Browser` K9's no-corruption promise is kept at, the comparison behind it staying that document's.

**To `Download` — the download primitives.** [Section 7](#7-staging-integrity-resume-and-publication--one-contract-in-both-directions) entire, which is the single answer Rows 208 and 209 need and which holds both of their pulling requirements: the partial survives a drop **and** never sits at the final name. The four values compared before a download resumes ([S13](#74-resume-never-trusts-a-partial-it-has-not-verified)), which is Fact 21 applied. The publication contract on the platform file adapter ([S18](#75-publication-is-the-last-step-and-it-is-one-step)). The per-transfer and whole-request reporting of [G6](#102-what-each-kind-of-work-has-to-report-and-from-where) for Row 299, **and [G4a](#102-what-each-kind-of-work-has-to-report-and-from-where)'s start-over mark, which is what lets a row still composing as waiting show a zero count without the zero being read as a restart nobody stated** (added 2026-08-21 for Row 415). **And [G4b](#102-what-each-kind-of-work-has-to-report-and-from-where)'s verifying mark: a resuming download's count does not move while its staged prefix is verified, which on a nearly complete prefix runs about twelve minutes, so that transfer is neither presented as stalled nor given a completion estimate composed as though it started at the checkpoint** (added 2026-08-23). **The record of what an arrangement has already fetched is Rows 212 and 217's and is that document's**, not the transfer record of S9. **Added 2026-08-31, at the first audited reading of this document against `File Browser`:** the presentation and explicit removal of a download's **local** staging orphans — staged material on the user's own computer whose transfer record is gone, which the desktop-side sweep declines to claim ([S28](#78-cleanup-and-the-five-ways-a-transfer-ends), second bullet). It was routed to `File Browser` until that date and that was wrong, because `File Browser` browses rented machines and disclaims every local surface; `Download` is the module that creates this staging. Also [S28a](#78-cleanup-and-the-five-ways-a-transfer-ends)'s ordering, on the same terms as for `File Browser`, and [S26b](#78-cleanup-and-the-five-ways-a-transfer-ends)'s rule that only *interrupted* means the work may be carried on.

**To `Render Settings`, `Rendering` and `Queue & History`.** The submission path's correlation, idempotency and three closed answers ([Section 4](#4-correlation-idempotent-submission-and-atomic-acknowledgement)), which is what makes Rendering's Rows 2 and 3 answerable and what Fact 8 turns on: the job identity is issued before the submission is sent, and a lost acknowledgement is resolved by asking the machine for that identity. Per-waiter deadlines and what expiry does not do ([4.6](#46-per-waiter-deadlines)) — **including, added 2026-08-29, that the supervisor-unavailable condition is raised on [C17a](#46-per-waiter-deadlines)'s verdict and never on a waiter's expiry alone, so `Queue & History` A3's *raised by the request not being answered* is that verdict consumed and a request left unanswered by the user's own dying line raises no verdict on the machine.** That a stop is its own command with its own identity ([C21](#47-cancellation-and-there-are-two-of-them)). The event journal as the reporting route ([G4](#102-what-each-kind-of-work-has-to-report-and-from-where)), read forward from a durable cursor. **And, added 2026-08-23, that no completion estimate composed over a transfer is composed as though a resume started at its checkpoint: a resuming transfer carries a verifying mark beside its count and moves no bytes while the mark stands** ([G4b](#102-what-each-kind-of-work-has-to-report-and-from-where)). **What composes the one queue, adoption, catch-up and what a history entry shows are `Queue & History`'s.**

**To `Job files and outputs`.** The staging-and-publication contract as the mechanism its atomic publication of a job's outputs uses ([Section 7](#7-staging-integrity-resume-and-publication--one-contract-in-both-directions)), including the same-filesystem requirement ([S1](#72-where-staged-material-sits-and-who-names-it)) and the declared degradation where the POSIX-rename extension is not announced ([S17](#75-publication-is-the-last-step-and-it-is-one-step)). **And, added 2026-09-03 at the code round, the bound on what the durability flush covers** ([S20a](#75-publication-is-the-last-step-and-it-is-one-step)) — the staged file and not the directory that afterwards holds the published name — which is the limit that document's M8 states for a job record and an attempt record. **The server directory layout, the job folder, and what a job's paths are called are that document's**; nothing here synthesises a path except the staging path of [S4](#72-where-staged-material-sits-and-who-names-it).

**To `Reporting a problem` — this document's diary declaration, and it is a declaration of none. Added 2026-08-31**, that document's [J2](Reporting%20a%20problem.md#3-what-goes-into-the-diary) requiring every feature document to declare in itself the acts it writes to the diary, and its [J4a](Reporting%20a%20problem.md#3-what-goes-into-the-diary) the machine endings and readings it writes as told lines. **This document writes no act lines and no told lines**, and that is a decision rather than a silence: the whole of the reason is that this document's unit is one transfer and no act the user asks for is one transfer.

- **The act the spec names for the transfer documents — *sent a batch* — belongs to the module that arranged the batch and not to this one.** J2 gives each of the spec's five acts *"to the module whose spec promises it"*, and **no spec promises this document's work**: it has no spec of its own, and every row it answers is a promise cross-filed here from `Whole app` or from the documents it supplies. What promises a batch send is `Dependency Upload & Remap`'s spec — *"Once the user is satisfied, Smoozy sends up everything in the **found** state"* — and `File Browser`'s for an ordinary upload. The line is theirs, one per send the user asked for; what this document supplies is the primitives that send is performed with ([Section 7](#7-staging-integrity-resume-and-publication--one-contract-in-both-directions)), which is the same split every other supply line above makes.
- **A line at this document's own unit would be one line per file, which [J2a](Reporting%20a%20problem.md#3-what-goes-into-the-diary)'s third test forbids outright.** The unit is one transfer — [T11](#64-the-transfer-support-workers-declarations-which-are-this-documents-and-had-not-been-made)'s *atomic or partial* declaration says so in as many words, *"the unit being one transfer"* — and one send is many of them: a dependency row may stand for a whole image sequence, a tile set or a cache series, and go up as one thing. So a line at [S26](#78-cleanup-and-the-five-ways-a-transfer-ends)'s ending, or at [S15](#75-publication-is-the-last-step-and-it-is-one-step)'s publication, is one per file inside one act the user asked for, which is the rate that test excludes by name. **The second test refuses it as well**: Row 281 promises what the user finds at the final name, and the completion a spec names is the send or the download, never the crossing of one file inside it.
- **The retries, start-overs and resumes are a level further in again, and none of them is a line either.** [S12](#74-resume-never-trusts-a-partial-it-has-not-verified) step 5's start-over, [S13](#74-resume-never-trusts-a-partial-it-has-not-verified)'s, [S12](#74-resume-never-trusts-a-partial-it-has-not-verified) step 3's verification and [C11](#44-re-establishing-after-a-connection-servers-replaced) step 4's resume after a repair are steps inside one transfer, which is itself a step inside one act; [S28](#78-cleanup-and-the-five-ways-a-transfer-ends)'s two sweeps are neither. **What already carries these to the user is a mark beside a count and nothing durable is written from it** ([G4a](#102-what-each-kind-of-work-has-to-report-and-from-where), [G4b](#102-what-each-kind-of-work-has-to-report-and-from-where)), and that is where they stay.
- **And no told lines.** J4a's declaration is of the machine endings and readings a document **consumes**, and every reading taken here — a listing, an attribute read, a free-space reading, a hardware reading, package inspection, a digest over a byte range ([G4](#102-what-each-kind-of-work-has-to-report-and-from-where)) — is taken for a consumer and is a step inside that consumer's act; where one reaches the diary it is written by the module whose act already read it, which is J4's own pattern for the machine's Blender version and its Linux family. **And [S26](#78-cleanup-and-the-five-ways-a-transfer-ends)'s five endings are not endings that came back from a machine**: they are Smoozy's own determinations, held on the desktop side in both directions for the reason [S7](#73-the-progress-record-is-on-the-desktop-side-in-both-directions) gives — on a full disk the machine cannot write the marker at all — so they are not that kind of thing to begin with.
- **What the declaration costs is said rather than left to be found.** A report covering a transfer that went wrong holds the arranging module's act line, whatever failure line was captured at the presentation of the failure ([J6](Reporting%20a%20problem.md#3-what-goes-into-the-diary)), and nothing between them about which file of the send it was. That is the trade J2a's third test makes everywhere, and this is the document where the count it refuses would be largest.

**To every document above — one rule stated once.** **No request, correlation, resumption, cleanup or trust decision in this protocol is keyed on a host key, on reachability, on a file's presence, on a file's name, or on a file's size.**

---

## 12. Unresolved

Each entry names what is not established, what breaks, and what holds without it. None of them stopped this document; [Section 13](#13-moments-that-reached-the-creator) records what was checked and did not reach a stop.

**The ruling of 2026-08-31, applied across this list rather than item by item. Added 2026-09-02.** The Creator ruled on that date that there will be **no further Blender research and no further rented machine for answering a question**.
