An item here whose only route was either is therefore **not waiting: it is permanently unresolved, it is not a pending item, and no reader may leave a space in the code or in the record for an answer that is coming.** **It reaches seven of the entries below and no others** — **1**'s two remaining halves, **3**'s four labelled residuals, **15**'s machine half and its platform half, **21**, **23**, **24**, and the head note's Windows throwaways above — each of which now carries the label where it stands, with what breaks kept in full. **Items 2 and 6 already carried it** and are the model the rest were brought to. **Five things it does not reach, named so that nothing is swept in that still has a route.** **Item 14**, which is *"HANDED TO BUILD, 2026-09-02, and deliberately not written as permanently unresolved"* with its reason stated. **Item 17**, which records a settled contradiction. **Items 4, 5 and 12**, which are closed. **Anything answerable by reading a publication**, which ruling or no ruling stays open and costs nothing — item 3's own history is four separate corrections got that way. **And the `File Browser` §13 item 9 ground** that item 15's machine half sits on, which is that document's to label rather than this one's. **A rental Build takes in order to run the application on is a different act and revives none of this.**

1. **Fact 78's publication interruption boundaries.** **ANSWERED, 2026-08-18, on ext4, xfs and overlayfs** — real forcible kills at each of the four commit boundaries left no part-arrived bytes at a final name, the pointer always named exactly one version, and every retry under the same identity resolved correctly ([The last two boxes](The%20last%20two%20boxes%20—%20the%20hook%20that%20cannot%20be%20read%20back,%20the%20full%20disk,%20and%20the%20unit%20that%20kills%20a%20render.md) §8). **What remains open, and permanently:** the rollback boundary was observed but never interrupted mid-flight; publication on a filesystem outside those three is untested; and Fact 44's genuine power cut remains unposable. **PERMANENTLY UNRESOLVED, 2026-09-02, on all three. These are not pending items and must not be read as ones.** The first two want a rented machine and the Creator ruled on **2026-08-31** that there will be **no further rented machine for answering a question** — and the rollback boundary was moreover blocked twice over on the machines that were rented ([Rented-server runtime](Rented-server%20runtime.md#16-unresolved) §16 item 3). Fact 44 was already correctly written as unposable and is **permanently unresolved unless the Creator takes the hypervisor route** (register, 2026-09-01), which is with them at `What Build inherits` item 4. **No reader may leave a space in the code or in the record for any of the three.** **Breaks:** [7.1](#71-the-contract-stated-once) clause 1 and [S15](#75-publication-is-the-last-step-and-it-is-one-step) on an untested filesystem or under genuine power loss — a publication interrupted at the rename boundary could leave part-arrived bytes at the final name, which is Fact 18's observed failure arriving through the one step that was meant to prevent it. And with it Rows 281 and 118 to 139's atomic publication of job outputs. The interrupted-rollback residual belongs to the package transaction and remains in [Rented-server runtime](Rented-server%20runtime.md#16-unresolved) §16 item 3. **What holds without it:** [State, identity and recovery](State,%20identity%20and%20recovery.md#96-where-a-unit-must-be-atomic-and-the-ground-for-it-is-not-established) §9.6 — the unit is written as atomic and this document says so; [S16](#75-publication-is-the-last-step-and-it-is-one-step)'s observation that the operation replaced an existing target wholly, with the inode changed and the staging name gone; [S20](#75-publication-is-the-last-step-and-it-is-one-step)'s ordering, which at least keeps a published name from standing over undurable bytes; and [S7](#73-the-progress-record-is-on-the-desktop-side-in-both-directions) to [S10](#73-the-progress-record-is-on-the-desktop-side-in-both-directions), which never read the final name as evidence of anything.
2. **Fact 79: the desktop-side publication operation, and it is now answered in part.** The fact is answered by two routes — each operating system's own documentation, and a throwaway using the chosen operation on a computer running that system — and **the documentation route is answered on all three systems**. **Mac:** `rename(2)`, macOS 26.5, page dated June 3, 2021, read 2026-08-17 — *"The rename() system call guarantees that an instance of new will always exist, even if the system should crash in the middle of the operation."* **Linux:** the same operating system's own `rename(2)` manual, quoted in Fact 78's answer — *"If newpath already exists, it will be atomically replaced, so that there is no point at which another process attempting to access newpath will find it missing"*. **The address is new to this document as of 2026-08-23**; the input document calls it *"the system's own manual"* and `Facts.md` Fact 78 repeats the words, both with no address, no page date and no reading date. Read **2026-08-23** at `https://git.kernel.org/pub/scm/docs/man-pages/man-pages.git/plain/man/man2/rename.2` — the Linux man-pages project's own manual source in its own repository. **The publication and the document agree word for word.** The page source carries no date of its own, its `.TH` line holding a build-time substitution rather than a date, and the same sentence stands in the tagged release `man-pages-6.05.01` at the same repository. **Windows is answered negatively on the documentation route and unread on the throwaway route**: its two published replace routes carry no crash guarantee, and one documents a failure state with the final name absent. **Nothing has been run on any computer**, which is what Fact 79's own *Machine* line means by *a rented Linux server does not answer for the desktop application merely by sharing an operating-system family*: the Linux throwaway wants a desktop Linux computer, and this project has none. **Windows's throwaway route was not posed, no Windows machine having been rented for it; its documentation route is answered negatively.** **Breaks:** [S18](#75-publication-is-the-last-step-and-it-is-one-step) for every download whose final artefact is a file on the user's own computer, and with it Rows 208, 209, 212 and 281 as the shape already states. **What holds without it:** §9.6 again, and the two requirements that do not depend on the platform — staging on the same filesystem, and no route to the final name but the publication step. **Permanently unresolved, 2026-09-02. This is not a pending item and must not be read as one.** The throwaway route wants two computers this project does not have — a desktop Linux computer and a Windows computer — and the Creator ruled on **2026-08-31** that there will be **no further rented machine for answering a question**. So the route is not late and nothing schedules it: it is shut, and Windows's documented negative is the standing answer rather than a placeholder for a run that is coming. **The silence is removed rather than waited out, and it costs no reading of its own.** Publication on the user's computer already proceeds on evidence that rests on no platform guarantee: [S15](#75-publication-is-the-last-step-and-it-is-one-step)'s pre-state records the staged object's byte length and SHA-256 digest before the final name is touched, its recovery branch converts absence into a fresh transfer from zero and never into success, and where the stable file identity is unavailable the byte length and digest are the recorded evidence and both must match. **On Windows specifically, `ReplaceFileW`'s documented failure state — the replaced file gone and the replacement under its original name — is met by that recovery branch as a mismatch**, so it ends in a restart rather than in a claimed publication. **What a builder may not do is add a Windows-only route to the final name, or read a successful return as a crash guarantee the vendor does not publish.** No new reporting channel is opened for this and none is wanted. **This is one of the eleven Windows items, and it says the same thing as [Desktop app and delivery](Desktop%20app%20and%20delivery.md#22-unresolved) §22 item 4, [Download](Download.md#15-unresolved) §15 item 18 and [State, identity and recovery](State,%20identity%20and%20recovery.md#13-unresolved) §13 item 3.**
3. **The extension set, the operation limits and the session cap on a rented machine's own file-transfer server.** [1.1](#11-what-the-surface-is-made-of-and-it-is-two-mechanisms-rather-than-one) and [1.2](#12-capability-negotiation-and-it-is-load-bearing-rather-than-a-courtesy) rest on `posix-rename@openssh.com`, `statvfs@openssh.com` and `fsync@openssh.com` being announced, on the reported operation limits, and on more than one subsystem session per connection. **ANSWERED on a rented machine by Fact 93, 2026-08-17, and this item's *on no rented machine* sentence was already false when it was written — corrected 2026-08-20.** Under the non-root login on a real rental, read off the wire by a protocol speaker: **the three extensions this document rests on are all announced**, in a set of eight; the operation limits were reported; and **the session cap is 10, per connection, with a second connection getting its own ten** — posed rather than trusted, ten of fourteen concurrent sessions on one connection succeeding with the first ten as the control. The eight are exactly the eight OpenSSH's own `PROTOCOL` file documents for the 8.9p1 release — **that release's own copy, read 2026-08-23 at `https://raw.githubusercontent.com/openssh/openssh-portable/V_8_9_P1/PROTOCOL`**, whose SFTP sections run 4.1 to 4.9 and whose extension sections name `posix-rename@openssh.com` (4.3), `statvfs@openssh.com` and `fstatvfs@openssh.com` together (4.4), `hardlink@openssh.com` (4.5), `fsync@openssh.com` (4.6), `lsetstat@openssh.com` (4.7), `limits@openssh.com` (4.8) and `expand-path@openssh.com` (4.9), which is eight. **Corrected 2026-08-23: the release's own copy had no address anywhere in this project and the only address carried was the master one, which is a different artefact** — master runs to 4.12 and adds exactly the three the 8.9p1 server did not announce, `copy-data`, `home-directory` and `users-groups-by-id@openssh.com`. The caller's eleven are those eight plus those same three; the shortfall is server-side copy, which no promise of this document's rests on and which costs `File Browser` M7 its preferred machine-side copy route (corrected 2026-08-31). `posix-rename@openssh.com` has been announced since OpenSSH 4.9 by that project's own release announcement of 2008-03-31, whose address and words [S17](#75-publication-is-the-last-step-and-it-is-one-step) now carries, so **no server of an age this project meets can lack it**. **The original reading stands only as history:** all of it was first observed on the caller's own end — macOS 26.5, `OpenSSH_10.2p1`. **One reading of [1.1](#11-what-the-surface-is-made-of-and-it-is-two-mechanisms-rather-than-one) is not on this item's ground and is named here so it is not swept in: the handle-lifetime result is observed on a rental** — [The multi-provider batch](The%20multi-provider%20batch%20%E2%80%94%20the%20surface%20that%20did%20not%20vary,%20the%20Debian%20route%20that%20exists,%20and%20the%20cards%20a%20slice%20cannot%20see.md) Test 4, 2026-08-22, with a control proving the channel alive and the handle refused (added 2026-08-23). The rentals on record announced older servers: `SSH-2.0-OpenSSH_8.9p1 Ubuntu-3ubuntu0.16` on the German box ([The host key](The%20host%20key%20—%20obtained%20without%20authenticating,%20and%20whose%20it%20turns%20out%20to%20be.md)). Two further hazards are documented rather than hypothetical: `sftp-server(8)` carries `-P denied_requests`, `-p allowed_requests` and `-R` read-only mode, so a provider image can ban a request or make the whole subsystem read-only, and `sshd_config(5)`'s `MaxSessions` can be set to 1 or 0 (both read 2026-08-17 on macOS 26.5; the `sftp-server(8)` page is dated July 27, 2021 and the `sshd_config(5)` page October 4, 2025). **And what `posix-rename@openssh.com` performs on the server is documented by OpenSSH itself, which this document had wrongly written off as a third instance of Fact 37's silence — corrected 2026-08-21.** The premise was true and the conclusion was not: there is no such **manual page** on this Mac, and OpenSSH does not put its extensions in manual pages. It puts them in `PROTOCOL`, the file that ships in every source release and opens *"This documents OpenSSH's deviations and extensions to the published SSH protocol."* Section 4.3, read 2026-08-20 at `https://raw.githubusercontent.com/openssh/openssh-portable/master/PROTOCOL`: *"On receiving this request the server will perform the POSIX operation rename(oldpath, newpath) and will respond with a SSH_FXP_STATUS message. This extension is advertised in the SSH_FXP_VERSION hello with version \"1\"."* **So [S16](#75-publication-is-the-last-step-and-it-is-one-step) is what somebody states it does, and not merely what it was observed to do** — and the chain closes end to end against a second first-party source, because [Section 12](#12-unresolved) item 2 already quotes Linux's own `rename(2)`: *"If newpath already exists, it will be atomically replaced, so that there is no point at which another process attempting to access newpath will find it missing"*. The version byte agrees too — the rental announced `data=b'1'` and `PROTOCOL` says version `"1"`. **This is not Fact 37's shape**, which is about diagnostic strings no source specifies; this is a wire operation OpenSSH specifies in the one file that carries all of them. **The habit worth keeping:** `statvfs@`, `fstatvfs@`, `fsync@`, `hardlink@`, `lsetstat@`, `limits@`, `expand-path@`, `copy-data`, `home-directory` and `users-groups-by-id@openssh.com` are specified in that same file, Sections 4.3 to 4.12 — every extension this document leans on is documented in the one file it had not read.

   **Widened to four boxes across three sellers, 2026-08-22, and the variance found is the useful part.** [The multi-provider batch](The%20multi-provider%20batch%20%E2%80%94%20the%20surface%20that%20did%20not%20vary,%20the%20Debian%20route%20that%20exists,%20and%20the%20cards%20a%20slice%20cannot%20see.md) ran the whole reading against three machines on three sellers, two operating-system families, two machine kinds, two login kinds and **three OpenSSH versions** — `9.6p1 Ubuntu`, `9.2p1 Debian` and **`8.9p1 Ubuntu`**. **Every load-bearing reading was identical on all three**: protocol version 3, max-packet **262 144**, max-read and max-write **261 120** each, max-open-handles **1 019**, `f_namemax` **255**, exclusive create refused over an existing path with a plain create succeeding as the control, base rename refused, `posix-rename@openssh.com` succeeding with the target holding the source bytes, `fsync@openssh.com` succeeding, session cap **10**, six parallel connections opened with no refusal, and 15 of 15 hostile filenames round-tripping. **One reading did vary and it is the tail:** the two 9.x servers announced **eleven** extensions and the 8.9p1 server **eight**, missing `copy-data`, `home-directory` and `users-groups-by-id@openssh.com`. **No promise of this document's rests on the three, and `File Browser` M7's machine-side copy prefers `copy-data` and declares its fallback (corrected 2026-08-31; this read *Smoozy uses none of the three*).** **That is what makes capability negotiation load-bearing rather than a courtesy** — an older but entirely ordinary rental is a real case rather than a hypothetical, and the rule that a capability not announced is never attempted is what makes it safe. **And max-open-handles is a per-machine reading rather than a constant**, on the same ground. **The 32 768 fallback branch of [1.2](#12-capability-negotiation-and-it-is-load-bearing-rather-than-a-courtesy) item 2 has now been unreached on four boxes across three sellers, and remains untested rather than disproved — permanently, 2026-09-02**, reaching it wanting a rental whose server announces no limits and the Creator ruled on **2026-08-31** that there will be **no further rented machine for answering a question**. **What holds:** the size is the protocol's own published recommendation rather than Smoozy's invention, and the caution that a **SHOULD** is not a guarantee is written into item 2 itself.

**Three further things are unestablished here and each is labelled rather than answered.** **All three, and the fourth at the end of the widening paragraph above, are permanently unresolved as of 2026-09-02: each wants a rental, the Creator ruled on **2026-08-31** that there will be **no further rented machine for answering a question**, and *parked with Fact 93* pointed at nothing in any case, that fact being answered. They are not pending items and no reader may leave a space in the code or in the record for their answers.**

- **F2's second route, and it is the one the mount resolution rests on.** Fact 28 established on a rental that free space can be read over the connection by an ordinary login, *"both through `df` and through the filesystem call underneath it"*. **What is not established is which program answers it on an arbitrary rental** — whether that program is present under the login Smoozy holds — **and whether its output survives Fact 37's caution**, which is that no manual documents what these tools emit, so a figure parsed out of it is being read from text nobody has specified. Label: **permanently unresolved, 2026-09-02** — it wants an arbitrary rental to be interrogated and there will be none. *(This read* **unresolved**, parked with Fact 93 *until that date, and Fact 93 is answered, so the parking pointed at nothing.)*
- **The fallback operation limits of [1.2](#12-capability-negotiation-and-it-is-load-bearing-rather-than-a-courtesy) item 2.** **Corrected 2026-08-21.** The pair of 32,768 bytes is **the file-transfer protocol's own published recommendation**, quoted at 1.2 item 2 from `draft-ietf-secsh-filexfer-02` Section 3, and not Smoozy's invention. What is unestablished is narrower and survives: the draft says **SHOULD**, so a server announcing no limits is recommended to accept that size and obliged to accept nothing. Label: **documented recommendation, not an observation, and permanently unresolved as an observation, 2026-09-02** — observing it wants a rental that announces no limits and there will be none. *(The words* parked with Fact 93 *stood here until that date and pointed at nothing, that fact being answered.)* **No observed machine has taken this branch** — the one rental interrogated announced its limits.
- **Whether a rental's session cap is zero**, which would refuse every shell and subsystem session; a cap of one is now handled by T5's alternating primary lease and T2's transfer-only additional connection. **Permanently unresolved as an observation, 2026-09-02** — meeting such a rental wants further rentals and there will be none. **What holds:** the setting is documented at `sshd_config(5)` and a cap of zero is already reported as an unsupported surface rather than discovered as a failure (item 5).

**This document exposed all of it and the register does not hold it.** **Breaks:** [S16](#75-publication-is-the-last-step-and-it-is-one-step) where POSIX-rename is absent (which [S17](#75-publication-is-the-last-step-and-it-is-one-step) already declares a degradation for); [F2](#91-a-reading-is-a-choice-of-mount-and-the-mount-is-the-one-the-write-was-attempted-on) and with it Row 184's reading and **route (a)'s** amount under Row 287 where **both** routes are absent — **route (b)'s reading is taken on the machine and does not break with them, corrected 2026-08-22 with [F6a](#92-the-two-routes-to-the-cause-and-the-moment-each-is-fixed-at)** — **and [F11](#93-what-the-reading-carries-and-how-far-it-may-be-trusted)'s resolution of a path to a mount, which rests on the second route alone and so is unavailable wherever that route is, whether or not the extension is announced; with it goes the one-condition-per-mount key [F13](#93-what-the-reading-carries-and-how-far-it-may-be-trusted) rests Row 288's *said once* on, which is `Desktop app and delivery`'s to apply. Added 2026-08-29: this line stated the cost of the unestablished ground as a free-space amount and named only F2's consumer, while F11's mount resolution fails on the strictly wider condition — a machine that announces `statvfs@openssh.com` and carries no answering program has a per-path figure and no mount**; [S20](#75-publication-is-the-last-step-and-it-is-one-step) where fsync is absent; every SSH use where the session cap is zero; and **every transfer to a machine that announces no limits**. **A cap of one no longer breaks command availability.** What holds without the remaining evidence is [1.2](#12-capability-negotiation-and-it-is-load-bearing-rather-than-a-courtesy)'s rule that a capability is never assumed, and the free-space second route remains strictly additional.
4. **CLOSED 2026-08-22 — the loop was run end to end over a real network to a rented machine, in both directions, on 4 GiB.** [The multi-provider batch](The%20multi-provider%20batch%20%E2%80%94%20the%20surface%20that%20did%20not%20vary,%20the%20Debian%20route%20that%20exists,%20and%20the%20cards%20a%20slice%20cannot%20see.md): **4 294 967 296 bytes** published in each direction by the loop described here — a chunk bounded by the server's announced max-write of 261 120, written at caller-chosen offsets into a staging path Smoozy names, a durable record updated every two seconds, resume verified by digest before a byte was added, publication by `posix-rename` as the last step. **Four independent digests agreed**, including the machine's own `sha256sum` — a program this project did not write. `fsync@openssh.com` returned OK; **the final name was absent right up to publication**, checked from the machine; **publication took 0.139 s**; and the staging path was gone afterwards, the rename being the cleanup. **[S15](#75-publication-is-the-last-step-and-it-is-one-step), [S16](#75-publication-is-the-last-step-and-it-is-one-step), [S20](#75-publication-is-the-last-step-and-it-is-one-step), [S22](#77-what-a-transfer-does-not-do-on-its-own-which-is-the-answer-to-the-second-clause) and [S26](#78-cleanup-and-the-five-ways-a-transfer-ends)'s Published row are observed rather than reasoned.** **What the transport did to the loop, which is what this item actually asked:** memory settled at **64 416 KB flat** across more than an hour of continuous transfer with **10 open file descriptors throughout** and the rate holding **384.0 KB/s to one decimal place**; peak resident size was 101 646 336 bytes on the upload and 117 178 368 on the download, **both dominated by the verification read-back rather than by the transfer**, which itself cost **967.4 s** to read 4 GiB back at 4.44 MB/s. **Two things the run found that the contract did not say and now does:** the write path must be pipelined ([R6a](#82-the-enforcement-point-is-smoozys-own-byte-loop-and-the-evidence-forced-it)) and the rate's averaging window must be bounded ([R6b](#82-the-enforcement-point-is-smoozys-own-byte-loop-and-the-evidence-forced-it)). ~~The original entry follows.~~ **Whether Smoozy's own byte loop behaves the same over a real network to a rented machine as it did over a pipe to a local server.** [R5](#82-the-enforcement-point-is-smoozys-own-byte-loop-and-the-evidence-forced-it)'s offset writes, staged partial, resume from a chosen offset and matching digest were observed **against OpenSSH's own server program driven over a pipe on the Creator's Mac on 2026-08-17**, with no network, no rented machine and no SSH transport between the two ends. **This document exposed it and the register does not hold it.** **Breaks:** nothing in the contract's shape, because every clause is about what Smoozy writes and where; what it leaves unobserved is the interaction of the loop with the transport — round-trip latency against the chunk size, the 1.0–1.4 s connection setup Fact 25 measured per stream, and whether a channel's write acknowledgements pace the loop in a way that changes the accounting interval. **What holds without it:** Fact 17's resume figures and Fact 75's cap figures, both measured over a real link to real rentals from this same home line; and the fact that the loop's correctness does not depend on its speed.
5. **CLOSED 2026-08-21 for `MaxSessions 1`, on both sides.** T5 gives the primary connection one alternating reading/command lease, commands pre-empt new readings, and T2 confines the additional connection to transfer-only use. No reserve is subtracted from a pool of one and a long-lived reading cannot starve commands. **`Servers` consumes that primary/additional connection contract at its §1.4 S14 and records it closed at its §16 item 10, on this item's own closing date**; the mechanism is settled here rather than left as its decision. **Corrected 2026-08-29: this read *one cross-document line is owed*, and that line had been carried on the day this item closed.** A configured cap of zero remains an unsupported SSH surface, already reported as capability unavailable. **One residual is not closed by this item and is recorded so nobody reads it as closed in both directions. Added 2026-09-02.** [T5a](#62-concurrency-and-one-admission-point-rather-than-one-per-server) narrows [The shape](The%20shape.md#7-desktop-app-and-delivery)'s *every per-server session-admission point*, and the shape's own sentence is still wider than T5a. That half is owed by the shape rather than here and rides [Desktop app and delivery](Desktop%20app%20and%20delivery.md#22-unresolved) §22 item 31. **Nothing in this document's mechanism moves with it** — the per-server point counts all three session kinds against one `MaxSessions` pool either way.
6. **The download side of the shared rate budget, and any mid-flight change of it.** Fact 75's shared-budget mechanism *"was tested on uploads only, and the download case differs materially — a receiver enforces a cap by not reading, so buffering can let the wire rate overshoot."* **The mid-flight change has now been run on the upload side — 2026-08-22 — and its half of this item closes; the download side does not.** Four runs, two governors ([The multi-provider batch](The%20multi-provider%20batch%20%E2%80%94%20the%20surface%20that%20did%20not%20vary,%20the%20Debian%20route%20that%20exists,%20and%20the%20cards%20a%20slice%20cannot%20see.md)): a **cumulative** governor answered 384 → 512 by bursting to about **1 000 KB/s** for minutes and 512 → 384 by falling to about **97 KB/s**, while a **bounded five-second window** landed 384 → 768 and 768 → 256 inside the minute each change fell in. **So the change does reach a running transfer, and only under a bounded averaging window** — [R6b](#82-the-enforcement-point-is-smoozys-own-byte-loop-and-the-evidence-forced-it) is the requirement that follows and [R12a](#84-the-achieved-rate-reading) is vindicated. **This clause used to read *a budget changed while channels are moving has never been run at all, in either direction*.** **The download half stands unrun**, and the receiver-side reason below is why it is a different question rather than the same one twice. **Breaks:** [R8](#83-a-cap-reaches-transfers-already-running-which-is-row-306), which is Row 306's answer, is a consequence of [R6](#82-the-enforcement-point-is-smoozys-own-byte-loop-and-the-evidence-forced-it) rather than an observation; and the down cap's fidelity to the wire, so [8.4](#84-the-achieved-rate-reading)'s reading may sit under the cap while the line carried more, which is exactly the *"judged against something real"* Row 289 asks for. **What holds without it:** the per-invocation result that forces the design (a cap held at 409 KB/s against a requested 400 on concurrent uploads to two boxes, and 402.8 and 405.5 KB/s on concurrent downloads, so **caps do hold in both directions**); the 421.9-against-800 forfeit that rules out per-transfer caps; and `sftp(1)`'s own `-l` flag being an invocation flag, which rules out the external-tool route by documentation rather than by measurement. **The download half is permanently unresolved, 2026-09-02. This is not a pending item and must not be read as one.** It wants a real capped download from a rented machine, and the Creator ruled on **2026-08-31** that there will be **no further rented machine for answering a question** — so it is not late and nothing schedules it. **The upload half stays closed by observation** and is not reopened by this: four runs, two governors, 2026-08-22, with [R6b](#82-the-enforcement-point-is-smoozys-own-byte-loop-and-the-evidence-forced-it) the requirement that came out of it. What stays unobserved is one thing only — **the down cap's fidelity to the wire** — and neither the enforcement point nor the shape depends on it ([R2](#81-where-the-budget-is-and-where-the-enforcement-is), [R15](#85-what-is-not-observed-and-it-is-named-here-rather-than-in-a-footnote)). **The silence is removed rather than waited out, and it costs no reading of its own**: [R15](#85-what-is-not-observed-and-it-is-named-here-rather-than-in-a-footnote) already states what [8.4](#84-the-achieved-rate-reading)'s downward reading counts — **bytes Smoozy accepted, never bytes the line carried** — so a download reading sitting under its cap is read as the cap holding at Smoozy's own accepting end and as nothing at all about the line. That is a bound on an existing reading rather than a new field, a new warning or a new channel. **What a builder may not do:** present a downward achieved-rate reading as the line's carried rate, or infer from it that a receiver-side cap held on the wire.
7. **Fact 44: what a machine losing power outright leaves.** **Not posable at all today**; the register says so and forbids putting it in a rental batch. The nearest observed case is a kernel-level reset which left *"no frame's contents at all"*, including a frame reported saved seconds earlier. **Breaks:** the assumption behind [S20](#75-publication-is-the-last-step-and-it-is-one-step) that a durable staged write is on the disk afterwards, and behind [S8](#73-the-progress-record-is-on-the-desktop-side-in-both-directions) that a record written before the first byte is readable later. **And [S17](#75-publication-is-the-last-step-and-it-is-one-step)'s compound case, which is the sharpest of the three**: a machine announcing **neither** the POSIX-rename extension **nor** the fsync extension takes the remove-then-rename route while S20's durability ordering does not apply, so power lost inside that window could take both the previous file at the final name and the staged replacement, and only Fact 44 says whether it does. **How reachable that case is was established on 2026-08-21 and it is close to nothing** — POSIX-rename has been announced since OpenSSH 4.9 and the oldest server on record here is 8.9p1 ([S17](#75-publication-is-the-last-step-and-it-is-one-step)), so *the sharpest of the three* is the least likely to be met. **It stays written**, because Fact 44 is unposable and an unreachable branch is not a repaired one. **What holds without it:** [S12](#74-resume-never-trusts-a-partial-it-has-not-verified), which verifies a staged prefix rather than trusting a record's number, so a record that did not reach the disk costs a transfer that starts over rather than a corrupt file; [7.1](#71-the-contract-stated-once) clause 1, which keeps the final name clean whatever the staged material turns out to be; and, for the compound case, [S15](#75-publication-is-the-last-step-and-it-is-one-step)'s pre-state, which makes the loss a recorded act naming both paths rather than a silent one — the source is on the user's own computer and the transfer is re-sent, so what the promise owes is the truth about what happened and that is kept.
8. **ANSWERED 2026-08-22, both halves, and both against the host key rather than for it.** A container's keys **do not** change without the hardware changing: the same offer re-rented presented three byte-identical keys, and a different machine on the same image presented three different ones — **they are the host's**, generated once and reused by whatever it rents out next. A virtual machine's **do** survive its recycle, because the image **ships** them: files dated months before the rental, byte-identical after a recycle that destroyed everything else on the box. **So on neither kind does an unchanged host key mean the machine is the same machine**, and [1.1](#11-what-the-surface-is-made-of-and-it-is-two-mechanisms-rather-than-one)'s prohibition is strengthened rather than qualified. **The break below cannot arise, because nothing here was ever keyed on a host key.** ~~The original entry follows.~~ **Whether a container's host keys ever change without the hardware changing, and whether a virtual machine's survive its own recycle.** Named in [The host key](The%20host%20key%20—%20obtained%20without%20authenticating,%20and%20whose%20it%20turns%20out%20to%20be.md) as not established. **Breaks:** nothing here, and it is recorded so no later reader repairs [A5](#3-the-authentication-boundary) by narrowing it. **Nothing in this document is keyed on a host key**, so both answers leave every rule above unchanged; the question belongs to Row 36 and `Servers`.
9. **Whether a scan of the offered host key can be defeated.** *"a middle that answers the scan honestly and the session dishonestly, or the reverse — was not posed and is not answerable from a single vantage point"*, and `ssh-keyscan(1)`'s exit statuses were observed rather than documented, the page having **no EXIT STATUS section at all** (Fact 82). **Breaks:** nothing in this document, which runs on a connection the trust decision has already permitted ([A6](#3-the-authentication-boundary)). It bears on Row 36 and is `Servers`' and `Security and licence`'s.
10. **Fact 8's full form, and Facts 40 and 41.** What a connection dropping mid-submission leaves on the machine, and whether the machine can be read for the work on it and its note per job, are posable only once the shape is implemented. **Breaks:** [C8](#43-atomic-acknowledgement-and-what-an-acknowledgement-is-not)'s three answers rest on the machine being askable for an identity, and [4.4](#44-re-establishing-after-a-connection-servers-replaced) step 3 on it answering. **What holds without it:** C8 needs only that an identity can be asked for, not that a note can be read in full; and the local record's pending-command record, written before the request leaves, which means the question can always be *asked* whatever the machine answers.
11. **Corrected 2026-08-22: the group of readings [1.1](#11-what-the-surface-is-made-of-and-it-is-two-mechanisms-rather-than-one) and [1.2](#12-capability-negotiation-and-it-is-load-bearing-rather-than-a-courtesy) rest on is now three sellers on one day, and item 3 carries what varied.** This entry used to read *one or two boxes on one or two days* of everything here without qualification, and that is no longer true of the surface. **It stays true of the rest**, and the caution is the point of the entry rather than the number. **Every claim about a rented machine in this document is one to four boxes on one to four days**, and the input documents disagree with each other in places — a refused copy left a zero-byte file on one box and no file at all on another (Fact 28, both readings), and the partial-holding directory appeared on one box after a hard kill and a randomly named temporary file on the other (Fact 17). **Breaks:** nothing named, because every rule above is written to hold on both kinds and the readings that could not be relied on are called out as traps rather than used. It is recorded so that no later document reads a figure here as a description of rented machines.
12. **Fact 29's name hazards are carried rather than closed.** All 33 hostile names reached the machine byte-for-byte, and coming back *"is where it breaks"* — `Capitals.txt` and `capitals.txt` collapsed to one file on the Mac, one tool aborted an entire transfer on a non-UTF-8 name where another skipped it, and a 255-**byte** name limit is 85 CJK characters. **Breaks:** nothing in [Section 7](#7-staging-integrity-resume-and-publication--one-contract-in-both-directions), because [S4](#72-where-staged-material-sits-and-who-names-it) keeps the staging path off the file's own name. **What it does not cover** is the **final** name, which is Rows 192, 205, 303 and 307's and belongs to `Desktop app and delivery`'s receiving-filesystem collision judgement and `Download`'s response to it. Named so nobody reads [S4](#72-where-staged-material-sits-and-who-names-it) as having solved the naming problem rather than kept it out of one mechanism. **A second in-Smoozy instance of the same hazard was observed on 2026-08-22 and it is on the machine side rather than the receiving side.** [The multi-provider batch](The%20multi-provider%20batch%20%E2%80%94%20the%20surface%20that%20did%20not%20vary,%20the%20Debian%20route%20that%20exists,%20and%20the%20cards%20a%20slice%20cannot%20see.md): **a directory listing that decodes entry names as UTF-8 raises** — `UnicodeDecodeError('utf-8', b'bad\xff\xfe.txt', 3, 4, 'invalid start byte')` — **while the write, the read-back over the subsystem and the machine's own `ls` all coped with the same name.** So the name is one Smoozy can move and cannot list. **The machine-side enumeration must carry names as bytes and decode only for display**, which `File Browser` X1 already requires of the local side and nothing required of this one. Owner: `File Browser`; it is recorded here because the enumeration runs over this document's surface.
13. **CLOSED 2026-08-21: the per-transfer record has one durable home and lifetime.** `State, identity and recovery` §1.1 names per-transfer records nested under their transfer arrangement or multi-part action outcome, including cleanup debts, and §11.1 gives those parent records their lifetime. S8 and S9 therefore have a store without inventing a second one.

14. **CLOSED 2026-09-09 at Build, step 10a piece E, and the constant does not move.** **What was run:** three real transfers through Smoozy's own byte loop over a real SSH connection on the loopback address carrying OpenSSH's own `sftp-server` — an upload of 256 MiB at 51.9 MB/s, a download of 256 MiB at 48.0 MB/s, and an upload held to the 620 KB/s [R17](#85-what-is-not-observed-and-it-is-named-here-rather-than-in-a-footnote) measured on a real rented line — **one in each direction, which is what this item asked for by name.** Each count series was taken at the accounting point itself and replayed through the real window at 250 ms, 500 ms, 1 s, 2 s, 3 s and 5 s. **What the figures said**, as the reading's standard deviation across the steady stretch with the widest spread between any two readings beside it: at **250 ms** 2.4 % and 1.106× up, 1.3 % and 1.044× down; at **1 s** 1.7 % and 1.068× up, 0.8 % and 1.027× down; at **2 s** 1.0 % and 1.033× up, 0.5 % and 1.019× down; at **3 s** 0.7 % and 1.020× up, 0.2 % and 1.007× down; and under a tenth of a per cent at every length at a real line's rate. **So *steady enough to read* is answered and does not decide the length — the figure is readable at every length tried, in both directions.** **What decided it is [R12a](#84-the-achieved-rate-reading)'s own lower bound**, which the same run turned from an argument into an observation: the three-second window was observed holding about six hundred counts at 51.9 MB/s, and at a slow single channel the floor is [R6](#82-the-enforcement-point-is-smoozys-own-byte-loop-and-the-evidence-forced-it)'s accounting period — so **one second holds about ten counts and one write moves the figure by a tenth, where three seconds bounds it at a thirtieth.** **What three seconds costs is [R10](#83-a-cap-reaches-transfers-already-running-which-is-row-306)'s own mixed reading for up to three seconds after a cap change, which that clause already declares.** **What was not watched and is not owed:** the two edges this item also names — the elapsed-time divisor at a transfer's start and the zero published when a direction's last channel ends — were exercised by the replay's own arithmetic rather than judged by eye, and no clause rests on how either reads. **The figures and the reasoning live at `THE_TRAILING_WINDOW` in `App/Desktop app and delivery/src/scheduling.rs`**, which is the one constant in the one place. **The original entry follows, struck.**

    ~~**[R12a](#84-the-achieved-rate-reading)'s three seconds was chosen and never watched.** Added 2026-08-21 with Row 329. **No achieved-rate reading has been drawn at any window length on any machine**, so *steady enough to read* is an argument from R6's sample count rather than an observation, and the same holds for how the figure behaves at the two edges the clause names — the elapsed-time divisor at a transfer's start, and the zero published when a direction's last channel ends. **Breaks:** nothing structural. The number decides only how the figure reads and how long R10's mixed reading lasts; every clause that consumes it — the sum per direction, the publication moments, the unit — is unchanged at any length. **What holds without it:** R12's count itself, which Fact 75's read half established at 409, 402.8 and 405.5 KB/s against known caps. **What would close it:** watching one real transfer at Build and moving one constant, which is where a number of this kind can be judged and here is not. **HANDED TO BUILD, 2026-09-02, and deliberately not written as permanently unresolved.** The Creator's ruling of 2026-08-31 — no further Blender research and no further rented machine for answering a question — **does not reach this item, and it must not be swept in with the items that ruling closes.** What is owed is **one reading, taken once, on any transfer Build already runs**: no rental, no research round, no fixture, no second machine. **What Build runs:** draw [R12](#84-the-achieved-rate-reading)'s achieved-rate reading over one real transfer in each direction at more than one trailing-window length — three seconds among them — and judge the figure against the two things R12a fixes the length for: whether it is steady enough to be read beside the cap, and how long it stays wrong after a cap change, a channel's admission or a channel's ending ([R10](#83-a-cap-reaches-transfers-already-running-which-is-row-306)). **What closes it:** either the three seconds stands, or the constant moves to the length that reads steady. **It is one constant in one place** ([R12a](#84-the-achieved-rate-reading)'s last bullet) — moving it changes no shape here, no clause that consumes the figure and no promise anywhere, which is why this is work rather than a silence. **Until it lands the three seconds is in force and every clause is unchanged at any length**, so nothing is blocked and nothing waits on it. **What may not be done:** no builder may adopt the five seconds [R16](#85-what-is-not-observed-and-it-is-named-here-rather-than-in-a-footnote) watched as if that run had judged this length — that run vindicated the trailing-window **shape** against the cumulative one and no length. **`What Build inherits` carries this as work Build does, not as a silence Build works around**, alongside the `Render Settings` §13 item 29 lookup.~~

15. **The staged and final objects' stable file identity, on both sides.** Added 2026-08-22. [S15](#75-publication-is-the-last-step-and-it-is-one-step)'s publication pre-state, recovery and rename check, [S28](#78-cleanup-and-the-five-ways-a-transfer-ends)'s ownership match and `File Browser` [X12](File%20Browser.md#44-the-partial-never-counted-row-197-and-row-191s-exclusion) and [M7](File%20Browser.md#74-copy-rows-192-and-197s-shape-machine-locally) all require it. On the machine it is a command-channel reading and sits on the same unestablished ground as [S13](#74-resume-never-trusts-a-partial-it-has-not-verified)'s four-value reading — revision 3's attribute structure carries no inode and no file identifier, and [`File Browser`](File%20Browser.md#13-unresolved) §13 item 9 owns that ground and now names these consumers. On the user's computer it is [S18](#75-publication-is-the-last-step-and-it-is-one-step)'s fourth requirement on the platform file adapter, and **no platform's answer has been demonstrated — permanently, 2026-09-02.** Demonstrating it wants a desktop computer running each of the three systems; two of the three are computers this project does not have, the Creator ruled on **2026-08-31** that there will be **no further rented machine for answering a question**, and the Mac third goes with them because the throwaway route is one question across three systems that can never be completed (`Facts.md` Fact 79). **The machine half is permanently unresolved on the same ruling**, wanting a rental whose command channel answers the stable file identity, with the ground itself owned by [`File Browser`](File%20Browser.md#13-unresolved) §13 item 9. **Neither is a pending item and no reader may leave a space in the code or in the record for either answer.** **The rule where the supplier cannot answer is S15's identity-unavailable branch, chosen over stopping publication**: publication proceeds on the recorded byte length and digest, which rest on no name; recovery permits `published` only on a full match of the recorded evidence — content proof where the identity was unavailable — and starts over where that proof cannot be composed; and S28's automatic removal, X12's transfer-aware delete verification and M7's copy-staging cleanup do not run against material whose identity was never recorded — the object stays visible as an orphan for the user's explicit removal, **through `File Browser` X12 on the machine and through [`Download`](Download.md) on the user's own computer** (S28's second bullet; corrected 2026-08-31, this having named the two machine-side clauses for both sides). The reading this replaced cost only a resume where it was unavailable (S13); stopping every publication in both directions to keep an extra check the content evidence already covers would have traded the whole transfer surface for nothing, and no spec grants that cost. **Breaks:** on a machine or platform that cannot answer, the automatic half of cleanup — abandoned staging accumulates until the user removes it by hand through X12's orphan route — and recovery's proof that a crash-interrupted publication happened, which then costs a transfer re-run from zero. The ownership guarantee itself — nothing is removed or published on the strength of a name alone — does not break on either branch. **What holds without it:** the staging path itself, which [S4](#72-where-staged-material-sits-and-who-names-it) keeps off the file's own name; [S12](#74-resume-never-trusts-a-partial-it-has-not-verified)'s verified prefix, which never trusts a record's number; and every publication, which proceeds on content evidence.

16. **B1's descriptor goes beyond the constraint it is written under, and `The shape` is closed.** Added 2026-08-22. [Section 5](#5-the-bootstrap-and-repair-request-and-package-inspection)'s Constraint quotes `The shape` exactly — the request *"carries a correlation identity, an idempotency key, and the requested package version"* — and [B1](#5-the-bootstrap-and-repair-request-and-package-inspection), directly beneath it, replaces the bare version string with the seven-field verified release descriptor and closes *"No request can select bytes by a version string alone."* The shape's wording cannot be kept: a version string names no bytes and anchors no trust, selecting bytes by one alone is exactly what [B8](#5-the-bootstrap-and-repair-request-and-package-inspection) to [B10](#5-the-bootstrap-and-repair-request-and-package-inspection)'s verified-release chain exists to forbid, and `Rented-server runtime` G26 to G31 already consume the descriptor. **Owner:** the architecture runner, to carry into `The shape` on the pattern this stage uses for a closed document a later answer outgrows; until it moves, B1 governs and the Constraint's quotation stands as what the shape says. **Breaks if the constraint is read literally:** a builder implementing its three fields alone builds the unverified request B10 forbids. **What holds:** everything else in Section 5 — the request is still one request, still authenticated by the connection, still idempotent against the package-phase record.

17. **SETTLED 2026-08-22: there is no wrapper, nothing of Smoozy's executes before hand-over, and the first Smoozy byte a machine ever runs is the package's own verified entry point.** Added 2026-08-22 as unresolved; settled the same day. The 2026-08-21 framing correction named one *"constant, installation-owned wrapper command"* as the carrier in both phases, which made a pre-package wrapper the first executable Smoozy puts on a bare machine — an artifact with no stated placement, no verifier and no trust anchor, in collision with `The shape`'s *"Before the server package exists, no Smoozy component is installed on the machine"* and this document's own component-free claim. **The resolution keeps the framing rule and removes the artifact, because the rule was a prohibition on interpolation and never a requirement for an installed file.** [1.1](#11-what-the-surface-is-made-of-and-it-is-two-mechanisms-rather-than-one) now splits the constant string by phase — after hand-over it is [B8](#5-the-bootstrap-and-repair-request-and-package-inspection)'s fixed entry-point name; before hand-over the channel carries only admissible commands under 1.1's rule, every value riding the subsystem's protocol fields, a constant command's standard input read as data, or the planted-link carriage. Package inspection became a composed subsystem reading in both phases ([B4](#5-the-bootstrap-and-repair-request-and-package-inspection)), the pre-hand-over at-rest digest a subsystem read-back ([S12](#74-resume-never-trusts-a-partial-it-has-not-verified) step 3, [S22](#76-integrity-what-is-compared-and-when)), and [B10](#5-the-bootstrap-and-repair-request-and-package-inspection)'s machine end the desktop's read-back comparison, with what that comparison is worth stated there rather than overstated. Both closed sentences stand unchanged and are now true. **What remained is carried rather than owed, and each line landed on 2026-08-22 — corrected 2026-08-29, this having gone on stating all three as owed, and part (a) having become false as written when the wording it attributed left the other document:** *(a)* `Rented-server runtime` G19, G26, G29 and G31 name B10's read-back comparison — performed by the desktop through the surface where no verified runtime is alive, and free to be the live verified runtime's own where one is — and the withdrawn pre-package-wrapper wording this part attributed to that document appears nowhere in it. *(b)* `Server Setup` R7a declares the planted-link carriage for the elevated steps that name a machine-origin path — its R7 hand-over of a chosen root above all — with its consuming command's link-treatment citation. *(c)* `File Browser` P6 carries the same rule for the elevated retries and M7 for the machine-side copy; item 9's which-program ground still carries their unestablished half. **Breaks: nothing.** Every command-side path on a never-held machine has a stated carriage, and what it stands on are the residuals already labelled — item 3's which-program-answers ground, item 11's one-or-two-boxes caveat, and item 9's output ground in `File Browser`. **Addendum 2026-08-22: the completeness sentence had one undeclared member, now declared rather than excepted.** `Rented-server runtime` K5/K6's lingering grant and read-back put the connection login's name — a user- or provider-origin identifier, and not a path — in a pre-hand-over command string, and none of the three carriages fits a non-path identifier consumed as an argument. [1.1](#11-what-the-surface-is-made-of-and-it-is-two-mechanisms-rather-than-one) now carries **machine self-derivation** as the fourth route: the command string stays constant and the machine derives its own login name at execution, cited to `sshd(8)`, the Shell Command Language and `id`'s own documentation, and K5/K6 use it and state what the derived name is and is not worth. The three-carriage enumeration earlier in this item stands as the record of the settling as first written; the rule's current form is 1.1's. Nothing else in this item moves.

18. **[G4b](#102-what-each-kind-of-work-has-to-report-and-from-where)'s three supply lines are recorded in [Section 11](#11-what-this-document-supplies-and-to-which-document) and were consumed by none of the three documents they name.** Added 2026-08-29. **Narrowed 2026-08-29 at the re-gate to the two that still carry nothing**, this having gone on stating all three as owed after `Download` had carried its share the same day. G4b was written on 2026-08-23 outside a round, and §11's lines to `Desktop app and delivery`, `Download` and `Queue & History` each state that a resuming transfer carries a verifying mark beside its count, is never presented as stalled, and has no completion estimate composed over it as though the resume started at its checkpoint. **`Download` M11a's third bullet, added 2026-08-29 at the stage close, carries it in terms** — the verifying mark and the prefix length it stands against, the twelve minutes on a nearly complete 4 GiB prefix at 5.9 MB/s, the stall test that does not fire, the inactivity deadline that does not fire, and the completion estimate that is not composed as though the resume started at the checkpoint — and `Download` §11's supply line to Design carries it too. **`Desktop app and delivery` and `Queue & History` still carry none of it, under that wording or any other**, and the item is those two. **Breaks:** `Desktop app and delivery` R15b's remaining-time estimate, whose own enumeration of what moves the figure names a cap change, a channel admitted or ended, a higher class taking admission order and G4a's start-over, and **not** a verifying resume — so on the words as they stand a resuming transfer is given an estimate composed as though it had started at its checkpoint, for the whole of a verification that on a nearly complete 4 GiB prefix runs **about twelve minutes** at the measured 5.9 MB/s while the count does not move and the recorded offset does not change ([7.4](#74-resume-never-trusts-a-partial-it-has-not-verified) S12 step 3). ~~And `Download` M11a's waiting-row composition, which separates a resume that keeps its figure from a start-over that does not and has no third state for a motionless verifying one.~~ **Struck 2026-08-29 at the re-gate: M11a's third bullet carries the third state, so that half is false as the tree stands. Nothing else in this entry moves.** **What holds without it:** G4b itself, which is complete on this side — the mark, its two moments, and the stall test and inactivity deadline that do not fire on it — so nothing here presents a verifying transfer as stalled at the layer that measures it. **Routed 2026-08-29**, one sentence to each of the three documents; **`Download`'s landed the same day and the other two have not.**

20. **The transfer-support kind's termination contract does not name X11's fourth thing — whether the worker outlives every member of its group.** Added 2026-08-29 at the stage-close gate repair, while writing [T11](#64-the-transfer-support-workers-declarations-which-are-this-documents-and-had-not-been-made). [C22](#47-cancellation-and-there-are-two-of-them) and [7.8](#78-cleanup-and-the-five-ways-a-transfer-ends) give the contract's other three — the target is staging and cleanup rather than a signal, at any moment, and S26 to S30 say what cleanup is owed — and `Rented-server runtime` X11 added a fourth on 2026-08-22 which this document has never stated. That document's **X1 names transfer-support in the list of kinds nothing states it for**, and its §16 item 6 already carries the general case. **Breaks:** X1 records the group leader alone and X9 reads a leader that is gone as a group that has ended, so on an unstated contract a live member of a transfer-support group reads as an ended group, X6 forbids relaxing the failed validation, and reconciliation writes *re-run once* over a transfer still moving — which on an upload is bytes paid for twice and on a resume is S12's verification run against a prefix another worker is still extending. **What holds without it:** the whole of [Section 7](#7-staging-integrity-resume-and-publication--one-contract-in-both-directions) — a re-run transfer stages under its own transfer identity ([S4](#72-where-staged-material-sits-and-who-names-it)), verifies before it resumes ([S12](#74-resume-never-trusts-a-partial-it-has-not-verified)) and publishes in one step, so the cost is duplicated work rather than a wrong final name. **Not written here rather than answered because it is outside this repair's one correction**, which was A1's declarations; the reading the evidence points at is that the worker's recorded execution group is the worker alone — the machine's file-transfer subsystem process is started by the machine's SSH server and never by the worker — and it is stated in this report for whoever carries it.
21. **Whether any provider but the one observed still routes a fresh connection to a rental's published address and port after a long stretch with no connection — which every step of [C11](#44-re-establishing-after-a-connection-servers-replaced)'s re-establishment presumes before its step 1 can run.** Added 2026-08-29, out of the scenario replay of *The connection drops while rendering*. The one observation is `Servers` [K5a](Servers.md#21-the-break-reporting-mechanism)'s: **3623 seconds** of complete silence, same host, same port, first packet — its own label is *exactly one hour, on one provider, on one rental*, it sets no upper limit, and the provider observed is not the one that scenario names; `Servers` §16 item 5 holds the neighbouring half, whether a seller later hands the same address to a **different** machine. **Breaks:** where a provider's mapping lapses under silence, every repair attempt fails even after the user's own line returns — the row reads the steady repairing presentation for as long as the application is open, `Servers` N8's *the hour costs the hour and nothing more* fails, the finished job's catch-up and the overdue-idle warning never arrive, the machine bills while idle, and the way back runs through the provider's console and possibly a host or port edit, outside anything these documents offer the user. Nothing lies to the user; the promise of automatic recovery is what fails. **Holds:** the sequence itself, which is right whenever a fresh connection arrives; and everything observed, on that provider, within that bound. **PERMANENTLY UNRESOLVED, 2026-09-02.** The Creator ruled on **2026-08-31** that there will be **no further rented machine for answering a question**, so there is no future rental batch and the per-provider question will not be posed: **this is not a pending item and no reader may leave a space in the code or in the record for its answer.** **What would have closed it, kept because the recipe is worth keeping and not because anyone will run it:** a per-provider question, posed blind with no expected answer written into it — what a fresh connection to a rental's published address and port meets after long stretches with no connection, on each of the other sellers in use. **What stands in its place** is the one observation, with its own label: `Servers` K5a's 3623 seconds of silence, same host, same port, first packet — *exactly one hour, on one provider, on one rental*, setting no upper limit — and C11's sequence, which is right whenever a fresh connection arrives and states no claim about whether one will.

What is owed there, beside its V1 or V2, is one line: that the moment an unanswered command-path request moves the observed value to *the supervisor not answering* is C17a's verdict, so a request left unanswered inside a break `Servers` then declares moves nothing. **Breaks:** a builder of the condition machinery reading that document alone raises on any unanswered request, and the dying line's request puts a machine verdict beside the connection-down statement for the whole outage — the exact fault C17a removes. **Holds:** the verdict itself, stated once at C17a, and `Queue & History` A3's consumption of it.

23. **[C17a](#46-per-waiter-deadlines)'s *a keepalive answered after the expiry says the line carried the silence* is an inference from `Servers`' declaration machinery and not an observation of the transport. Added 2026-08-29 at the re-gate. Label: unresolved.** The clause is cited to `Servers` [K2](Servers.md#21-the-break-reporting-mechanism) and [K3](Servers.md#21-the-break-reporting-mechanism), which are mechanism clauses — K2 names the two mechanisms that report a break, K3 fixes the figures at a 15-second interval, three unanswered and a declaration between **45 and 60 seconds** — and the observation under them is Fact 57, which measures **detection latency** (a caller's own line pulled, detected in **56 seconds** with keepalives on and **not at all for 21 minutes 45 seconds** with them off) and says nothing about whether a transport carried a request sent earlier in an interval that ended with an answer. **What is unestablished is exactly that step**, and it is the step that decides whether a machine verdict is raised. **Breaks:** a transport interruption shorter than K3's own declaration bound — the line falters, the waiter expires, the line returns, the next keepalive is answered — moves the observed value to *the supervisor not answering* over a fault that was the line's. That is a machine verdict earned by nothing, which is the exact fault C17a exists to remove and which `Preparation/Specs/Whole app.md` forbids in terms (*"A lost connection is a fact about the connection, never a verdict on the machine."*), arriving on a narrower window than the one C17a closed; and the window is not a hair's breadth, K3's bound being 45 to 60 seconds. **What holds without it:** C17a's shape and its first, third, fourth and fifth bullets, untouched; the waiter's expiry still raises nothing, which is [C17](#46-per-waiter-deadlines) standing whole; a declared break still discards the observation; and the third arrival added to bullet 2 on 2026-08-29 — the request's own late answer — discards it too, so a supervisor answering late no longer leaves the condition to be raised behind it and what remains exposed is the sub-declaration interruption alone. **Not answered here, and permanently unresolved as of 2026-09-02.** It is a question about the transport, posable only on a rental whose line can be interrupted for a bounded stretch shorter than the declaration bound and then restored — and the Creator ruled on **2026-08-31** that there will be **no further rented machine for answering a question**, so **there is no future rental batch to owe it to: this is not a pending item and no reader may leave a space in the code or in the record for its answer.** *(This read* it is owed to a future rental batch as a blind question *until that date.)* **What would have closed it:** exactly that, posed blind and never settled from memory (Rule 2) — the recipe is kept because it is the only honest one, not because anyone will run it. **The label C17a's first bullet carries stays *unresolved* and now means permanently so.** **No answer moves, no row reopens, and the verdict stated at C17a stands** — what is exposed is the sub-declaration interruption alone, and it is exposed permanently.

24. **The stat case has been posed on the caller's own end and on no rented machine. Added 2026-08-31, at the first audited reading of this document against [`File Browser`](File%20Browser.md). Label: unresolved.** The two status codes and their meanings are **documented**; that a real server returns them at an attribute request is **observed**, twice, with a control on each side and a stated negative, and [1.3](#13-what-the-surface-does-not-carry) now carries both labels. **What is not established is the same thing item 3 records for every other reading of this surface**: the runs were on the Creator's Mac driving its own file-transfer server over pipes, and no rental has been posed at this case, so a provider image whose server answers differently — item 3 already names `-P denied_requests` and read-only mode as documented ways an image alters the subsystem — would not have been seen. **The stale evidence account this item routed to `File Browser` P1 was repaired on 2026-09-01**, with that document and its Sources companion now naming the machine, software version, controls and negative. **This item remains open only on its original, narrower ground: no rental has been posed at the stat case.** **Breaks:** on a rental whose server answers otherwise, `File Browser` P1's separation at the stat case, and with it that document's rule that **no message anywhere may say the two cannot be told apart**. **What holds without it:** everything in this document. 1.3's own binding consequence is which channel a request needing the three told apart is carried on, and that rests on the readdir separation and on the codes being defined at all; no promise here reads a stat status. **Not answered here, and permanently unresolved as of 2026-09-02:** it is a question for a rental, on item 3's ground, and the Creator ruled on **2026-08-31** that there will be **no further rented machine for answering a question** — so it is shut rather than owed, **not a pending item, and no reader may leave a space in the code or in the record for its answer.** It is never settled from what a server is expected to do. **What would have closed it:** the same stat case posed on a rental, with the controls and the stated negative the caller's own end already carries. **What holds:** the two status codes and their meanings are documented, a real server returning them at an attribute request is observed twice with a control on each side, and [1.3](#13-what-the-surface-does-not-carry) carries both labels.

That clause reads the marks and never the bar's direction, which is Row 415's own ground, and it says in terms that no mark of its own is invented and G4a's is not withdrawn. **Nothing in this document moves**: the mark is supplied exactly as [Section 11](#11-what-this-document-supplies-and-to-which-document) has named it since 2026-08-21, and the count of supplies owed is now one shorter rather than one short. *(This item's assertion that* `File Browser` names neither G4a nor a start-over mark anywhere *was true when it was written and stopped being true on 2026-09-02.)* ~~The original entry follows.~~ **[G4a](#102-what-each-kind-of-work-has-to-report-and-from-where)'s start-over mark is supplied to `File Browser` and consumed by nothing in that document. Added 2026-08-31, at the first audited reading of this document against it.** [Section 11](#11-what-this-document-supplies-and-to-which-document) has named the mark to `File Browser` and `Dependency Upload & Remap` since 2026-08-21; `Download` consumed it at M11a and `File Browser` names neither G4a nor a start-over mark anywhere, while its U11 sums the per-transfer counts of G4 into one whole-request bar. **So on the screen where a File Browser upload request is one row with one bar, a unit that starts over drops the whole's bar with no mark and no word behind it**, which is what Row 415 exists against. **The mark should be consumed rather than withdrawn**, and the reason is Row 415's own: from a falling count alone a transfer that has just begun and one that has begun again are the same report, and that is as true of a whole as of a unit. **It is not withdrawn here and no second mark is invented**: the mark is this document's, the whole is `File Browser` U11's, and the consumption is one clause in that document. **Breaks:** nothing of this document's; what breaks is Row 415's promise at the whole-request level in `File Browser`. This is [item 18](#12-unresolved)'s shape for G4b applied to G4a, and it is recorded so the count of supplies owed is not one short.
