## 13. Moments that reached the Creator

~~**None from this document.**~~ **CORRECTED 2026-09-27: one, [B10a](#5-the-bootstrap-and-repair-request-and-package-inspection), and it reached the Creator as their own ruling rather than by the escalation below.** It narrows how far B9's anchor reaches, which is a security clause, and the build runner's R5 had sent exactly that up rather than take it. Every other answer in this document stands as *none*.

Eighteen answers were checked against [Method](Method.md)'s third escalation — does this put anything in front of the user that no spec says they get, a place to look, something to type, or a thing that can be lost. None reached it, and the checks are recorded because no gate catches this one. **Raised from six to eight on 2026-08-29 at the re-gate.** The two clauses written on 2026-08-29 outside a round — [C17a](#46-per-waiter-deadlines) and [T11](#64-the-transfer-support-workers-declarations-which-are-this-documents-and-had-not-been-made) — were not in the count and not in the list, which is the one way a passage written outside a round falls out of this section without a gate noticing.

**Raised from eight to fourteen on 2026-09-02, and the fault is the identical one a second time.** Six more clauses have been written outside a round since — [S4](#72-where-staged-material-sits-and-who-names-it)'s attempt number, [S26a](#78-cleanup-and-the-five-ways-a-transfer-ends)'s reversed first bullet, [S26b](#78-cleanup-and-the-five-ways-a-transfer-ends), [S28a](#78-cleanup-and-the-five-ways-a-transfer-ends) and [S31](#77-what-a-transfer-does-not-do-on-its-own-which-is-the-answer-to-the-second-clause) on 2026-08-31, and [S26c](#78-cleanup-and-the-five-ways-a-transfer-ends) on 2026-09-02 — and none was in the count or in the list. **All six were checked on 2026-09-02 and none reaches the escalation.** **S4's attempt number** is a component of a path nobody is shown, on a machine the user never opens; nothing can be lost by it, the superseded numbers being retained in [S9](#73-the-progress-record-is-on-the-desktop-side-in-both-directions)'s record precisely so the sweep can reach the paths they named. **S26a's reversal** changes what a user sees and changes it by withdrawing a false statement: an integrity failure no longer shows as *waiting for the connection* on a live line, and no whole file is silently re-sent. Its staged material is removed, which is the one thing on this list that could look like a loss and is not — [S24](#76-integrity-what-is-compared-and-when) has already established those bytes are wrong, and the alternative is resuming over them. **S26b** is a bar on presentation and adds nothing to any screen. **S26c** fixes the scope of one word for builders and adds no record, no field and nothing shown; the two senses the user meets are each already composed by the document that owns them. **S28a** requires a record to be written to *stopped* before a replacement is arranged, which puts abandoned staging on the sweep's list — the cost is Smoozy's own material on the user's rental, weighed in this section's abandoned-staging paragraph below, and the opposite leaves every leftover partial on the rental for the life of the installation. **S31** is a negative: nothing is compressed, so the user meets no new thing at all, and the clause exists so that a reader looking for the negative finds one.

**Raised from fourteen to fifteen on 2026-09-06, and this time the clause was added to the list in the sitting that wrote it.** [B8a](#5-the-bootstrap-and-repair-request-and-package-inspection) was written that day outside a round and it does not reach the escalation. It names who serves a released server package and it names nothing else. The fetch it describes is the desktop application's own, made under `Desktop app and delivery` E10 and E11 with no account and with nothing of the user's in the request; the user is not present at it, is not shown it, is given nothing to look at and nothing to type by it, and can lose nothing through it. **What the clause withholds is an address**, which is a value the Creator holds rather than anything the user meets.

**Raised from fifteen to sixteen on 2026-09-19, the clause again added to the list in the sitting that wrote it.** [B8b](#5-the-bootstrap-and-repair-request-and-package-inspection) was written that day outside a round and it does not reach the escalation. It says where the desktop reads the current release file from, and the read is the same unattended fetch B8a describes: the user is not present at it, is not shown it, is given nothing to look at and nothing to type by it, and can lose nothing through it. **The one way it can go wrong is weighed and is not a loss**: a wrong *latest* hands over an older signed release, which B9's floors refuse or which is merely not the newest.

**Raised from sixteen to eighteen on 2026-09-27, one clause late and one on time.** [B8c](#5-the-bootstrap-and-repair-request-and-package-inspection) was written on 2026-09-26 outside a round and **was not added here in the sitting that wrote it** — the fault this section has recorded twice before. **B8c does not reach the escalation.** It changes how the artifact is built and which machines it runs on, and it widens that set rather than narrowing it; the user is not present at the build or the placement, is shown nothing and asked for nothing, and loses nothing. Its one cost is a larger artifact on the user's own line once per placement, which B8c itself names. **[B10a](#5-the-bootstrap-and-repair-request-and-package-inspection) was written on 2026-09-27 and is checked here in the same sitting.** It does not reach the escalation either: no release build has any route to it, so no user ever meets it. **It reached the Creator by another road** — it narrows where B9's anchor applies, a security clause, and the Creator's ruling of that day is its authority, quoted in the clause.

**The declared degradation of [S17](#75-publication-is-the-last-step-and-it-is-one-step).** Where a machine's file-transfer server does not announce the POSIX-rename extension, publication over an existing name becomes remove-then-rename, and there is a window in which neither version is at the final name. **The considered alternative was to refuse transfers to such a machine, and it was declined for this clause** — a rental Smoozy declines is a cost no spec grants. The degradation is not a thing that can be lost: the staged file is whole, the record names both paths, and the window is recoverable on the next connection. **It is also not the hazard the promise is about**, which is a hybrid or a part file presenting as whole, and remove-then-rename cannot produce either.

**The two routes of [F6](#92-the-two-routes-to-the-cause-and-the-moment-each-is-fixed-at) and the filing of [F17](#94-what-the-carve-out-may-not-claim). Route (b)'s moment moved on 2026-08-22, scenario replays Pile 1 item R11, and the check was taken again over the answer as it now stands.** The carve-out fires on a refusal Smoozy's own write met, or on a free-space reading against the failed job's own output path taken on the machine in the act that writes that render's recorded outcome — and on nothing looser. **The user meets no new thing and loses nothing the promise grants**: route (b) is what keeps the case [Whole app](../Specs/Whole%20app.md) puts inside the promise, and the moment is now pinned at the ending itself rather than at whatever later minute a desktop connects. **What the correction gives back is the promise's own case.** With auto-download off and Smoozy closed, freeing the disk first is the ordinary shape, and under the old moment that act made the reading roomy and the diagnosis silent — the user meeting exactly the failure the carve-out was written for and being told nothing about it. **The residual was weighed and left, and it is narrower than the one weighed before**: a mount something else had filled by the moment the outcome was recorded is reported as having no room, which is true of the place, is said alongside the machine's own words, and never becomes a claim about why the render ended ([F14](#94-what-the-carve-out-may-not-claim)) — Row 287's *alongside* wording permits exactly that sentence. **The opposite ruling would have been the escalation**: a cause named from a reading at any later moment would sometimes tell the user to free space over a failure that had another cause, which is the app being wrong on screen and is never past the line. **And the reading's own age is not that escalation either**, because what it is attached to is a recorded ending rather than a live condition ([F11a](#93-what-the-reading-carries-and-how-far-it-may-be-trusted)) — the spec settles that itself, *"freeing the disk does not put the frames back that were never rendered"* — so a disk freed afterwards leaves a true statement about a past moment standing rather than a false statement about the present. F17's refusal-driven message is not this carve-out at all and is filed against the spec's general known-cause rule, so no user-facing behaviour turns on the filing.

**The mid-transfer cap behaviour of [R10](#83-a-cap-reaches-transfers-already-running-which-is-row-306).** A cap lowered while transfers run governs forward and cannot claw back bytes already sent, and a reading over the change window reads between the two rates. It is not a place to look, something to type, or a thing that can be lost, and Row 289's own promise is about what Smoozy uses from that moment rather than about a retrospective total. **What the user is shown while a cap is taking effect is Design's**, and Row 306's answer is what that decision was waiting on.

**The resume that starts over of [S12](#74-resume-never-trusts-a-partial-it-has-not-verified) step 5.** Where a staged prefix does not verify, the transfer starts again from zero and the work already done is spent. **The cost is time on the user's own line and never a file**, and the alternative is the one OpenSSH warns about in its own words — a resumed file that *"is likely to be corrupt"* with no error, which would be Row 281 broken rather than a cost. Row 281's own wording forecloses the trade: *"the user is never handed something that looks finished and is not."*

**The pre-hand-over read-back of [S12](#74-resume-never-trusts-a-partial-it-has-not-verified) step 3 and [B10](#5-the-bootstrap-and-repair-request-and-package-inspection).** Before hand-over an upload's staged prefix and a staged package artifact are verified by reading their bytes back over the subsystem rather than by a machine-side program, and the read-back is time on the user's own line. **The cost runs the cheap way**: the bytes cross downward at several times the upload rate the alternative start-over would re-spend — **about six to one on the later of the two readings of this line ([R17](#85-what-is-not-observed-and-it-is-named-here-rather-than-in-a-footnote)) and 11–12 on the earlier (Fact 25), and the weighing is taken at six, corrected 2026-08-23** ([S12](#74-resume-never-trusts-a-partial-it-has-not-verified) step 3) — and a package artifact crosses back once per placement. The alternative — a Smoozy verifier placed before the package exists — is the contradiction [Section 12](#12-unresolved) item 17 records as settled, and it would have verified nothing anyway, being itself unverified. Not a place to look, something to type, or a thing that can be lost.

**The abandoned-staging sweep of [S28](#78-cleanup-and-the-five-ways-a-transfer-ends).** It removes material inside Smoozy's own staging directories on a machine the user rents, without asking. It reaches nothing the user put there, it is bounded to paths [S4](#72-where-staged-material-sits-and-who-names-it) names, and leaving it undone would spend the user's disk indefinitely on material no record names. **The opposite ruling would have been closer to an escalation** — abandoned staging accumulating on a rental until the disk filled is a failure the user meets and nobody wrote down. **The identity-unavailable branch of [S15](#75-publication-is-the-last-step-and-it-is-one-step) was checked here too** ([Section 12](#12-unresolved) item 15): on a machine whose command channel cannot answer the stable file identity, the sweep does not run and staged leftovers stay until the user removes them through the visible orphan route — weighed against stopping every publication on such a machine, which would be the larger cost and one no spec grants.

**[C17a](#46-per-waiter-deadlines) and [T11](#64-the-transfer-support-workers-declarations-which-are-this-documents-and-had-not-been-made), both written on 2026-08-29 outside a round and both checked on that day's re-gate.** **C17a only withdraws.** It removes a machine verdict the user was being shown on a silence the transport never carried, and adds no place to look, nothing to type and nothing that can be lost; where it changes what the user sees, the change is one fewer statement about a machine, and the connection condition's own honest statement stands where it stood. **T11 is never presented.** It declares a resource class, a priority, cancellability, an atomic-or-partial boundary and a machine-level exclusion for the transfer-support kind, all of them read by the machine's arbitration and none of them shown, on a machine the user never opens; and its *cancellable at any moment* was checked for a cost and there is none, A6 cancelling only an operation that holds what an exclusive operation needs and this class holding nothing exclusively. **Neither reached the escalation.**

---

## 14. What this document does not own

Cited, and deliberately not restated or decided here.

- **When a connection is made or replaced, the connected period, reconnect policy, how many repair attempts and how long between them, host trust's user-facing half and the changed-identity warning** — `Servers`, Rows 24 to 46.
- **The machine-local endpoint's existence, lifecycle and access control; boot registration; the complete package placement, commit, rollback, health exchange, repair and replacement transaction and its durable phase record; per-worker termination contracts and signal targets **less the transfer-support kind's own, which is [C22](#47-cancellation-and-there-are-two-of-them)'s and [T11](#64-the-transfer-support-workers-declarations-which-are-this-documents-and-had-not-been-made)'s** (**narrowed 2026-08-29 at the re-gate**: the bullet read wider than C21, C22 and T11, and that document's X11 puts each contract with the document that defines the kind); scheduling and exclusion; captured streams and the explicit collection outcome; the machine-held answer-release path; the setup-in-progress state** — `Rented-server runtime`, Row 286.
- **The local access boundary at the endpoint, the secret store, the local record's protection, the crossing lists, the erasure order, logging redaction** — `Security and licence`, Row 278. The **inbound boundary at a rented server** is that document's §1.3 and nothing here hardens it.
- **Every identity's issuer and rules, the register, desired versus observed state, the event journal and acknowledgement, the operation record and the four outcomes, the deliberate stop's sequence, reconciliation on both sides, failure records and their two kinds, the atomic-or-partial boundary, tombstones, and the local lifetimes** — `State, identity and recovery`, Rows 285 and 292.
- **The one shared upload and download rate budget, the local database and its migrations, the platform adapters, the platform-file publication operation and its per-system demonstration, receiving-filesystem collision judgement, the automatic supervisor-unavailable repair trigger, the presentation of every failure, partial outcome and progress state, the repeat-suppression rule of Row 288, and the update pipeline** — `Desktop app and delivery`, Rows 280, 282, 284, 288, 290, 303 and 304–305.
- **The user-facing fallback re-run for package repair, readiness and runtime-health inspection, Blender and add-on installation, restart, resumable steps, privilege escalation and safe re-run** — `Server Setup`, Rows 88 to 117, 295, 283 and 308–309.
- **The server directory layout, the submission manifest, the job folder and asset area, path and name rules, output redirect, remap material, the expected-output inventory and the already-there test a re-run reads** — `Job files and outputs`, Rows 118 to 139 and 297.
- **The pool size and the priority order among auto-download, manual download and upload; the already-exists check; the record of what an auto-download arrangement has already fetched; what a request as a whole is counted in; and the user-facing response to a part-arrived file** — `File Browser` and `Download`, Rows 180 to 220, 298–299 and 307. **Checked 2026-08-29 and left as it stands.** The `File Browser` gate routed a correction here — that the `File Browser` half of this locator undercounts by nine, **Rows 340 to 346, 428 and 486** being that document's too. **All nine are Design rows**, and every bullet of this list names its documents' spec rows plus the spec rows cross-filed to them and omits their Design rows — this list's own locator convention, which `Render Settings` §15 states in terms. So **none of the nine are owed here** and the span is unchanged; what a bullet gives is a locator for the topics named, not an exhaustive ownership claim.
- **Atomic job submission and the launch contract; stop, re-run and carry-on as user actions; what composes the one queue, held jobs, adoption, catch-up and what a history entry shows** — `Rendering` and `Queue & History`, Rows 1 to 23, 47 to 87, 294 and 302.
- **Which key types are offered, what makes a key usable, and the fingerprint mechanism** — `Keys`, Rows 241 to 254 and 293.
- **The single receiving slot, cross-machine routing, and everything the Telegram worker sends or receives** — `Telegram`, Rows 255 to 277 and 296. Its worker submits commands through the same supervisor command path this protocol frames, and nothing here gives it a route of its own.
